# Landscape survey — raw agent output (2026-08-18)

Produced by a 50-agent workflow; **27 agents completed**, 21 failed on a weekly
usage limit and 2 were lost to a script bug (`parallel()` received promises
instead of thunks, since fixed). This file is the raw salvage so the findings
are not stranded in a transcript directory.

Every citation here is an agent's claim. Agents were instructed to verify papers
by fetching abstracts and to mark anything unverifiable `UNVERIFIED` rather than
invent an identifier — but **nothing below has been independently checked by the
maintainer yet.** Treat arXiv IDs as leads to verify, not as established facts.

Completed: 25 territory surveys, 2 of 8 gap analyses.

---

## Gap analyses (complete)

# TERRITORY VERDICT: Systems, Kernels & Serving

**Headline: the codec is gone, the selection rule is wide open, and the single best experiment in this territory costs almost nothing because the data already exists.**

---

## 1. What is genuinely unsolved, and which asset fits

**(a) Nobody knows whether per-layer KV sensitivity is additive — and two shipping systems bet that it is.**
NVIDIA ModelOpt #2211 "solves a width-weighted **additive** recipe." HIGGS's DP allocator assumes additive layer-wise L2 error. The researcher's own coordinate is *worst-layer* attention-logit correlation — a **max**, not a sum. If damage is max-dominated rather than additive, every additive allocator systematically mis-spends bits: it will trade away the one layer that matters to buy margin on twelve that don't. **Asset: the 96-configuration / 13-model damage-law dataset, already collected.** This is re-analysis, not new GPU time. Nothing else in the territory has the dataset to test it.

**(b) Hadamard rotation cannot remove a shared mean, and the entire field is currently acting as if it can.**
llama.cpp merged auto-rotation (PR #21038, 2026-04-01); vLLM's TurboQuant uses WHT + sign flips; int4_per_token_head uses Hadamard pre-rotation; KVarN uses Hadamard + variance normalization. An orthogonal rotation maps N(μ,Σ) → N(Hμ, HΣHᵀ): the offset survives with its norm intact, and a Lloyd-Max codebook fitted on N(0,σ²) is then aimed at the wrong place. The public evidence is already sitting there unexplained: llama.cpp discussion #23470 measured **PPL 1589.9 vs 7.96 (×199.7) on Qwen2.5-7B with rotation ON**, K-side only, V nearly free. And PR #21038's own table shows rotation helps asymmetric q4_1 by ×9.5 on Qwen3-0.6B but symmetric q4_0 by only ×1.34. **Asset: the per-head shared-mean mechanism + ρ measurements on 16 models.** This is the one asset with no upstream counterpart, and it explains someone else's published anomaly.

**(c) The 200× contradiction between the two most-cited llama.cpp measurements is unreconciled.**
PR #7412 (2024, the folklore source): q4_0 K costs ×1.03. Discussion #23470 (2026): ×199.7. Both authoritative, both quoted, nobody has explained the gap. **Asset: the 176-cell atlas across 26 models / 13 lineages.** The atlas says damage is a model property spanning ×0.99 to ×1348 — which is exactly the shape of that contradiction. This is a motivating figure that writes itself and belongs to no one else.

**(d) No stack has a model of how KV damage scales with context length.**
vLLM's own blog concedes it verbatim ("errors accumulate with sequence length"), llama.cpp tracking issue #24139 is open and unanswered, and KVarN's entire thesis is decode-time accumulation. **Asset: 22 measured noise-response curves**, currently at a single context. A length term is a genuine extension of the law into a gap two ecosystems have stated in their own words.

**(e) Damage prediction for non-perplexity observables.**
llama.cpp issue #25725: DFlash draft acceptance collapses 99% → 0–2% under a q8_0 draft cache **on an RTX 4090**, restored by `LLAMA_ATTN_ROT_DISABLE=1`. vLLM #40831/#52475: MTP × TurboQuant produces degenerate token loops silently. Nobody can predict either. If score-space noise predicts acceptance collapse the way it predicts PPL damage, that is a second axis with zero competition.

**(f) Pre-deployment safety checking.** Both stacks have documented *silent* quality failures (DeepSeek-V4 gibberish, MTP loops, Gemma-4 five-gate stack, acceptance collapse). vLLM's docs are still FP8-only; users are publicly asking which models even work. **Asset: kvcheck.py + the atlas.** Low novelty, high utility, high visibility.

---

## 2. Solved or about to be — do not touch

- **The codec.** WHT/Hadamard + Lloyd-Max is merged in vLLM (`turboquant_*`, 2026-04-15) *and* llama.cpp (auto-rotation, 2026-04-01) *and* `int4_per_token_head` (2026-06-24). Dead as a contribution.
- **Compression ratio.** Settled at 2.6–4.9×. "5.54×" is no longer a headline; it is a table cell that invites the throughput question you cannot answer.
- **Throughput/kernel work.** vLLM's own open checkbox is "CUDA/cutedsl kernels to replace Triton." That is a team-scale race against a Huawei lab (KVarN) and vLLM core. Unwinnable solo.
- **New quantizer designs.** Variance normalization (KVarN), query-subspace orthogonality (SQuat), per-token-head zero-points (#40835). All contested or merged.
- **Rotation improvements.** ggerganov pre-emptively insulted this exact PR class ("the incoming flood of vibe generated PRs implementing TurboQuant").
- **MLA support.** Another solo contributor already has a TRITON_MLA_TURBOQUANT prototype on 4× 4090.
- **Bit-packing.** Engineering, not research.

---

## 3. Ranked proposals — impact × P(one 4090) / effort

**#1 — "Layer sensitivity is not additive" (re-analysis, ~1 week, no new GPU time)**
*Measure:* on the existing 96 configs, fit (i) additive per-layer sensitivity, (ii) max-over-layers, (iii) top-k mixtures; compare predicted vs measured perplexity damage. Then simulate ModelOpt's and HIGGS's allocators against the true damage and report the bits they waste.
*Publishable if:* additive fits materially worse than max-dominated, especially in the >×5 regime. That is a direct correction to two shipping systems (ModelOpt #2211, HIGGS DP solver) using data neither has.
*Kills it:* additivity fits fine. Then say so — it is still a useful validation, but the differentiation collapses to "absolute magnitude + catastrophe gate," which is thinner.

**#2 — "Rotation is mean-blind": the controlled 2×2 in llama.cpp (~2 weeks)**
*Measure:* {rotation on / `LLAMA_ATTN_ROT_DISABLE=1`} × {symmetric `q4_0`/`q5_0` / asymmetric `q4_1`/`q5_1`} × {high-ρ vs low-ρ models, ≤14B}, reporting PPL, KLD, and score-space noise. Then the injection experiment: add a synthetic shared per-head component to an immune model and show rotation fails to rescue it while an asymmetric type does.
*Publishable if:* ρ predicts the rotation × symmetry interaction. This explains discussion #23470's ×199.7-with-rotation-on, explains PR #21038's own q4_1-vs-q4_0 asymmetry, and yields a one-line deployment rule ("high ρ → use `q4_1`, not `q4_0`") that the ecosystem can act on today. It also falsifies SCJedi's competing "linear-attention layers act as error correction" explanation (issue #21385) by breaking a hybrid model on demand.
*Kills it:* ρ does not predict the interaction, or `q4_1`'s per-block min already absorbs the mean well enough that the effect is small. Both are cheap to find out.

**#3 — The sequence-length term (~3–4 weeks)**
*Measure:* noise-response curves at 2k/8k/32k on models that fit, plus the accumulation regime KVarN describes. Test whether one curve plus a length factor predicts long-context damage.
*Publishable if:* a single extra parameter generalizes. This is the limitation vLLM states and cannot predict, and it is the axis KVarN built a whole paper on.
*Kills it:* injected static noise and autoregressively accumulated error are different processes and the curve does not transfer. Real risk. A clean negative is still worth a section, not a paper.

**#4 — Predicting draft-acceptance collapse (~1–2 weeks)**
*Measure:* reproduce #25725 on your own 4090; regress acceptance rate against score-space noise across cache types and models.
*Publishable if:* the same coordinate predicts a second observable. That is the strongest possible evidence the coordinate is right rather than fitted, and it explains a live open bug in both stacks.
*Kills it:* acceptance is dominated by draft/target mismatch rather than KV damage.

**#5 — kvcheck + the per-model support-and-safety matrix (~2–3 weeks)**
*Deliverable:* a one-forward-pass gate that reports predicted damage and refuses above threshold, plus the model matrix vLLM users are explicitly asking for (#40069, gaby's comments; PR #45422 still open).
*Useful if:* it catches ≥1 of the known silent failures (MTP loops, DeepSeek-V4, Gemma-4) before a user sees output. High community leverage, low academic novelty. Ship it as the companion artifact, not the paper.
*Kills it:* nothing, really — worst case it is a well-received tool.

**#6 — Out-of-tree mean-removal KV format in vLLM (high effort, defer)**
Feasible: `register_backend(AttentionBackendEnum.CUSTOM)` + `AttentionSpec.state_content_bytes`/`customize_spec` + Triton JIT on the precompiled wheel (`VLLM_USE_PRECOMPILED=1`), no CUDA build. `fp8_ds_mla`'s 656-byte composite slot is the template. The genuine hook: compressed-tensors is symmetric-only and carries per-head *scale* but no per-head *offset* anywhere in the checkpoint path — a code-level statement of an unserved need. But do this only *after* #2 proves the mean matters. Otherwise it is a kernel project with no result behind it.

**Do not do:** any project whose headline is compression ratio, throughput, or a new quantizer.

---

## 4. Collisions that sink a novelty claim

1. **FATAL — "TurboQuant" is vLLM's.** Merged 2026-04-15, same name, same components (WHT + Lloyd-Max), four presets, blog post, eval suite. **Rename the project before writing another sentence.** Any framing as "a from-scratch TurboQuant KV implementation" reads as a fork of an upstream feature.
2. **FATAL to rotation novelty** — llama.cpp PR #21038 (2026-04-01) made Hadamard KV rotation the *default, silent* baseline.
3. **SEVERE, one day old** — ModelOpt #2211 + vLLM #52813 ship layer-wise KV allocation from per-layer sensitivity. Cite and distinguish on four axes (search vs closed-form; relative KL ranking vs absolute predicted multiplier with error bars; additive assumption you can refute; ≥4-bit only, never enters the catastrophic regime). Ignoring it is the likeliest desk-reject.
4. **SEVERE** — HIGGS (arXiv:2411.17525) already pairs a noise-calibrated law with a per-layer DP allocator. Weight-only, quadratic, valid above ~3 bits. Say the difference in the first paragraph — especially because vLLM brands its backend "**TurboQuant/HIGGS**," so this audience already conflates them.
5. **SEVERE** — SQuat (COLM 2025, vLLM PR #44564) owns query-subspace-orthogonal error. Mandatory citation for the score-space coordinate, and it means "per-layer adaptation on top of TurboQuant" is taken.
6. **SEVERE** — `int4_per_token_head` (merged) carries a real asymmetric zero-point with a score-side `sum(Q)*zp` correction. **"vLLM cannot handle the key offset" is now false.** Lead with the *diagnosis and injection experiment*, never the fix.
7. **MODERATE** — llama.cpp discussion #23470 and issue #21385 publicly stake "K catastrophically more sensitive than V" and "architecture determines tolerance," dated April–May 2026. Your value is explanation and prediction, not observation. Present them as your evidence, not your discovery.
8. **CLEAN** — the cosine-similarity null result (Pearson −0.003), the score-space coordinate applied to KV, the catastrophic regime, and the ×5 gate have **no counterpart in either stack**. Nothing upstream calibrates by quantizer-free noise injection. Open with the null result; it is directly falsifying for the reconstruction-error framing every merged PR implicitly assumes.

---

## Feasibility and hygiene notes

- The local venv is **vLLM 0.19.1, which predates the entire TurboQuant merge**. Upgrade before making any reproducible claim about `turboquant_*`.
- **SM 89:** FlashAttention refuses fp8 KV (`flash_attn_supports_fp8()` requires FA3 + capability family 90). Route through `TRITON_ATTN` or `FLASHINFER`.
- **Audit the atlas for rotation contamination.** llama.cpp auto-enables Hadamard rotation silently whenever the cache type is quantized and `head_dim % 64 == 0`. Any llama.cpp-derived measurement straddling 2026-04-01 is not comparable to itself.
- **Internal inconsistency to resolve before publishing the mechanism:** the asset list claims exact decomposition into "k_proj bias at boundary layers"; the project's own August preprint-track memory records the k_proj-bias hypothesis as **refuted**. Pick one and make sure the paper states the current one.
- **Coverage limit to concede up front:** calibration still requires running the model, so ≤14B in 4-bit. You cannot validate on Qwen3-30B-A3B or Llama-70B. Frame for the consumer-GPU/llama.cpp constituency — which is real, vocal, and underserved (vLLM issue #41403) — and let method transfer be the claim rather than model coverage.
- **Measure your own throughput once, honestly, and stop leading with compression.** KVarN's RFC has already installed the field's reflex: "it costs throughput and accuracy... losing both is why the feature stays off." A 5.54× headline with no throughput column walks straight into it.

# TERRITORY ASSESSMENT: Architecture Trends (MLA + Attention Normalization)

## Verdict in one line

**The MLA half is mostly a trap; the QK-Norm half contains the single best experiment available to this researcher in any territory surveyed so far.** Ai2 built and released the exact confound-free instrument the damage law needs (OlmPool: 26 pretrained 7-8B models varying only four architecture knobs) and explicitly declined to measure quantization. Go there. Treat MLA as a one-week generalization section, not a project.

---

## 1. What is genuinely unsolved that our assets uniquely fit

### 1a. Nobody has ever measured whether an architecture choice changes KV quantizability, under control. (Asset #1 + #4)

Every paper in the normalization slice recites the same unmeasured chain: QK-Norm → weaker logit growth → weaker sinks → sinks believed to hurt quantization → therefore QK-Norm models quantize better. The only public statement of the full claim is a HuggingFace deployment blog from 12 days ago, and it is an *attribution*, not an experiment. OlmPool holds data, tokenizer, optimizer, token budget, and scale fixed and varies QK-Norm, norm ordering, KV-head count, SWA, and pretrain context length. The 176-cell atlas structurally **cannot** answer this — 26 models across 13 lineages means lineage confounds everything. OlmPool is the only dataset where the confound is gone, and the noise-response curve is the only tool that produces a *continuous, quantizer-free* damage measurement you can regress a knob against.

The fit is exact and slightly uncanny: the damage law needs a per-model curve; OlmPool gives 26 models whose differences are known by construction. The curve's parameters become the dependent variable. Nothing else in either survey has this property.

### 1b. The sharp, falsifiable prediction that makes it a thesis rather than a sweep

QK-Normed MLA (2606.16310) gives the algebra: modern QK-Norm is **RMSNorm on head_dim — it rescales but does not center**. After normalization ‖k̂‖ ≈ ‖γ_k‖, so per-token scale becomes nearly free, but the learned per-channel gain re-imposes fixed channel anisotropy and **nothing removes a per-head shared mean**. That is precisely the seam asset #3 sits in.

Prediction, pre-registerable: **QK-Norm collapses the per-token norm variance but leaves ρ (mean-dominance) untouched. If ρ is what drives catastrophic KV damage — which asset #3 verified causally by injection — then QK-Norm should NOT improve KV quantizability.** That contradicts the field's standing belief, contradicts the HF blog attribution, and is consistent with the blog's own anomaly (Gemma-3 *has* QK-Norm and still degrades). Confirming or refuting it is a controlled experiment nobody has run.

### 1c. Can the damage curve be predicted instead of measured? (Asset #1 → its strongest possible upgrade)

Today the law requires a noise sweep per model. On OlmPool you can ask whether curve parameters (slope, catastrophe-threshold noise level) are predictable from cheap static statistics — ρ, γ_k anisotropy, massive-activation magnitude, sink rate — *without* running the sweep. Regressing that on 26 lineage-confounded atlas models is meaningless. Regressing it on 26 identically-trained models is legitimate. This converts the asset from "a calibration procedure" into "a zero-shot predictor," which is a categorically stronger paper and directly upgrades kvcheck.py.

### 1d. The Qwen confound must be repaired, and OlmPool is the only repair

The Qwen3 tech report states verbatim that Qwen3 removed the Qwen2 QKV-bias **and** introduced QK-Norm *in the same release*. The mechanism asset's headline ("immune → catastrophic at Qwen2 → immune by Qwen3.5") is therefore confounded at the vendor source. Any reviewer who reads 2505.09388 finds this in five minutes. OlmPool varies QK-Norm with no bias change — it is the disambiguating instrument, not a nice-to-have.

### 1e. (Smaller, real) Head-shared error vs per-head independent error. (Asset #1)

MLA's two algebraically equivalent decode paths touch different tensors. Quantizing a 512-dim head-shared latent injects error every head sees through its own W_UK — structurally correlated across heads. Quantizing reconstructed per-head K injects independent errors. Nobody has measured whether identical *score-space* noise from these two sources produces identical damage. If it does, the score-space coordinate survives a genuine structural stress test. If it does not, you have found an honest boundary of your own law — which is publishable and far better than having a reviewer find it.

---

## 2. What is solved, or about to be — do not touch

- **"MLA has heterogeneous quantization sensitivity; the decoupled RoPE block is the fragile part."** SnapMLA claims this explicitly as a first (Feb 2026). Taken.
- **"Treat RoPE dims differently from content dims in MLA."** DeepSeek-V4 ships exactly this policy inside the checkpoint. Proposing it is proposing the frontier default.
- **FP8 KV on MLA.** SnapMLA is the reference implementation and the vendor default. Dead ground.
- **RoPE-block bit allocation on TurboQuant-MSE.** Block-GTQ (2606.24033) owns it, on a ten-model panel, with a logit-space fidelity metric and a catastrophe-avoidance headline.
- **WHT + 3-bit packed KV cache as a systems contribution.** RotaryQuant is ten days old with near-identical shape, and vLLM already ships four TurboQuant KV dtypes with published PPL deltas. **Asset #4's value is now as an instrument, not as a product.** Its 5.54x is uncomparable until a PPL delta is attached next to vLLM's 4.9x/+20.59%.
- **Architectural KV compression itself** (MHA2MLA, TransMLA, X-EcoMLA, CARE, Attention Editing). All require training budget. Out of reach, permanently.
- **QJL / residual signs.** vLLM's own config docstring states a 5-group community consensus that it hurts attention quality. That is a measurement obligation, not an argument to win. Either produce numbers or drop it silently.

---

## 3. Ranked proposals

**P1 — Architecture × KV-quantizability, controlled (OlmPool).** Impact high × P(works) ~0.85 / effort medium (3-4 weeks). **Do this.**
- *Measure*, per checkpoint (26 models, pre- and post-64K-extension): the model's own score-space noise-response curve; ρ per head per layer; massive-activation magnitude; sink rate and attention entropy (cross-validate against their published values); real quantizer damage at 2/3/4-bit; and whether the curve predicts the quantizer cells. Dependent variable = curve parameters. Regressors = the four knobs, then the static statistics.
- *Publishable if*: knob → curve-parameter effects are significant with interpretable sign. **Strongest outcome**: QK-Norm helps quantizability while OlmPool's own headline says it hurts long context — a priced architecture tradeoff nobody has quantified. **Second-strongest and more likely per 1b**: QK-Norm has no effect on KV quantizability, killing a field-wide belief and a live practitioner hypothesis with the only controlled data that exists.
- *Kills it*: (i) **disk/bandwidth** — 26 checkpoints × 2 stages × ~15-30 GB. Check total footprint and streaming feasibility *before* anything else; this is the single highest-probability failure mode and it is not intellectual. (ii) Ai2 or a reader of their blog runs it first — it is their obvious follow-up and the barrier is low. Move now.
- *Not a killer*: "all 26 models are robust at 3-bit." The noise curve spans catastrophe by construction; you do not need the quantizer to break anything.

**P2 — MLA generalization section: immunity test + shared-latent amplification.** Impact medium × P ~0.8 / effort low (1 week). **Do this, as a section of P1's paper, not standalone.**
- *Measure*: ρ on c_KV and on W_UK-reconstructed per-head keys for DeepSeek-V2-Lite and Moonlight-16B-A3B (the only two 4090-runnable native MLA checkpoints, identical 512+64 geometry to V3). Test the RMT paper's implicit prediction that decoupled-RoPE MLA is constitutionally immune to normalized-key collapse. Then inject mean-free noise at the latent vs at reconstructed per-head keys, calibrated to *identical* worst-layer score-space noise, and compare damage.
- *Publishable if*: it produces the first noise-response curve for latent attention, and either confirms MLA immunity-by-construction or exposes the head-correlation limit of the score-space coordinate.
- *Kills it*: n=2. Which is exactly why it is a section.

**P3 — Composition grid: architectural compression × post-hoc quantization.** Impact high × P ~0.4 / effort medium. **Gated on a 20-minute check.**
- *Go/no-go*: do MHA2MLA / TransMLA / X-EcoMLA / CARE release converted **checkpoints**? If conversion requires fine-tuning, this is dead on arrival — no training budget.
- *Measure*: latent width × bit width, including the Int2 row that exists nowhere in the literature. Ask whether the two mechanisms consume the same score-space budget (prior: sub-additive) and whether the damage law predicts joint cells from single-axis calibration.
- *Kills it*: no released checkpoints; or "converted models are proxies, not real MLA."

**P4 — vLLM pluggable-KV-dtype landing (RFC #51751 / PR #52670) for an MLA backend.** Utility, not research. Real, dated, unclaimed opening — every MLA backend caps at FP8 today. But it is a large engineering lift validated on a two-model panel. Opportunistic only; do not spend research time here.

**P5 — Sub-8-bit MLA RoPE-block quantizer.** **Do not lead with this.** The arithmetic is seductive (the 64 RoPE dims are 40% of a 3-bit compressed MLA cache; attacking them is worth 1.48x) but it is triple-collided (SnapMLA diagnosis, DeepSeek-V4 policy, Block-GTQ method), the panel is n=2, the vendor baseline is already FP8 not BF16 so marginal gain collapses from ~5x to ~2x, and there is no serving path in vLLM. Bad risk-adjusted return.

---

## 4. Collisions that would sink a novelty claim

1. **Block-GTQ / RoPE-Aware Bit Allocation (arXiv:2606.24033, 23 Jun 2026) — the dangerous one.** Built on TurboQuant-MSE, ten-model diagnostic panel, per-layer MAE on RoPE QK logits (a score-space metric on a multi-model panel), headline = catastrophe avoidance (51.7 vs 0.0 collapse). Four-axis adjacency. **"Score-space fidelity on a multi-model panel predicts catastrophic KV quantization failure" is no longer a clean claim.** Your surviving differentiators are narrow and must be stated explicitly: quantizer-free noise injection, a calibrated continuous curve with an R² and a held-out bit-width test, and a catastrophe *gate* with a measured zero-dangerous-miss rate. Read it before writing a word.
2. **HuggingFace FP8-KV blog (2026-08-06)** states the exact hypothesis "Qwen2-family lacks QK-Norm → key-cache outlier channels → KV quantization collapses." Dated, indexed, public. Asset #3's headline is no longer a surprise. Reframe as: *this is the practitioner hypothesis; here is the controlled test it lacks* — and note it already fails its own monotone story (Gemma-3 has QK-Norm and still degrades).
3. **Qwen3 Technical Report (2505.09388)** — QK-Norm added and QKV-bias removed in the same release. Confounds the Qwen-generation narrative at the source. Unfixable without OlmPool.
4. **SnapMLA (2602.10718)** — explicit first-claim on MLA sub-block quantization sensitivity.
5. **CARE (ICLR 2026, 2603.17946)** — independent, accepted, partial priority on both "reconstruction error in the wrong coordinate is uncorrelated with real damage" and "uniform per-layer allocation is wrong." Cite as convergent evidence, do not claim either as novel.
6. **HIGGS (2411.17525)** — prior art on noise-insertion calibration; already known, but its ">3 bits only" restriction is the sentence your catastrophic-regime claim must lean on, verbatim.
7. **RotaryQuant (2608.08081)**, **HyperQuant (2606.23406)**, **eOptShrinkQ (2605.02905)**, **NOVA-KV (2608.04074)**, **TurboAngle (2603.27467)** — all direct competitors to asset #4 as a *quantizer*. TurboAngle in particular collides with layer-adaptive allocation and per-model sensitivity profiling. Reposition asset #4 as measurement apparatus and the collision surface mostly disappears.

---

## Assumptions and caveats

- The second survey (attention normalization) was truncated mid-entry; the Wortsman/small-scale-proxies entry and anything after it are incomplete. The OlmPool, QK-Normed MLA, Qiu et al., Sun et al., Softpick, and HF-blog entries were complete enough to reason from, and P1 rests on those.
- I did not independently verify OlmPool's release footprint, license, or checkpoint sizes. **That verification is the first action item and it can kill P1 on logistics alone.**
- MHA2MLA/TransMLA converted-checkpoint availability is unverified and is the sole gate on P3.
- No files were changed; this is analysis only.

---

## Territory surveys: collision risks and author-stated open problems

### Evaluation, metrics and benchmarks for KV-cache compression — specifically: which error measure people use as a stand-in for real model damage (per-vector MSE/cosine, attention-score/logit error, Fish

**State of the art.** Three separate "best" answers exist, and no one has unified them — which is the gap.

1) BEST PROXY FOR ATTENTION-LEVEL DAMAGE: score-space / softmax-Fisher error, from HeadQ (arXiv:2605.03562, withdrawn). The defining numbers: Fisher/score-space error vs attention KL reaches Spearman 0.696–0.997 across six models, versus 0.031–0.376 for raw key MSE — a ~0.62–0.69 Spearman gain. Value side: A²-weighted MSE vs output MSE Spearman 0.984, vs 0.746 for A-weighted and 0.492 for raw value MSE. Note this predicts attention KL, not perplexity, and is rank correlation, not calibrated magnitude.

2) BEST PREDICTOR OF PERPLEXITY FROM A DISTORTION MEASURE: the HIGGS linearity theorem (arXiv:2411.17525, NAACL 2025) — layer-wise L2 error maps linearly to PPL increase, coefficients fit by multi-level Gaussian noise insertion with no quantizer. Defining limit: valid for b > 3.0 bits and "diverges on lower bitwidths"; weights only.

3) BEST GROUND-TRUTH SUBSTITUTE IN PRACTICE: KL divergence to the FP16 reference. Academic anchor: "Accuracy is Not All You Need" (arXiv:2407.09141, NeurIPS 2024), KL vs flip rate correlation ~0.96–0.98. Practitioner anchor: token-by-token top-40 KL over ~250k tokens (localbench, Apr 2026). Best published head-to-head of proxies for bit allocation: KL beats SQNR beats MSE, Kendall tau ~0.79 vs ~0.76 (arXiv:2604.13440) — a margin small enough that the question is not settled.

NUMBERS THAT DEFINE THE LARGEST PUBLISHED PAIRED PANELS in this slice: 11 models × 5 benchmarks × 1,894 prompts (Alignment Collapse, safety axis); 6 models × {2,3,4}-bit × 4 ranks (HeadQ, attention-KL axis); 6 methods × 3 models × 185k evaluations (Query-Visibility audit, ranking axis); 5 models 124M–14B (Quantization-Dominates-Rank-Reduction, PPL axis); 4 methods × 2 models × 4 tasks (Agrawal & Mayer benchmark). NOBODY publishes an R² for predicting an end-to-end perplexity damage MULTIPLIER from a KV-space proxy, and nobody publishes a false-negative rate at a catastrophe threshold. Those two numbers are currently unclaimed.

**Collision risk.** RANKED, MOST DANGEROUS FIRST.

1) HeadQ, arXiv:2605.03562 — SEVERE, PARTIALLY SINKS ASSET #2's FRAMING. It already publishes, on six models, that "Fisher/score-space error predicts attention KL far better than raw key MSE", with the Spearman numbers to back it. The researcher flags it as known prior art, but is likely under-weighting it: their asset-#2 headline ("cosine is uncorrelated with real damage; attention-logit correlation is better but insufficient") is the SAME QUALITATIVE CLAIM in a different coordinate. The paper being withdrawn does not protect us — it is indexed on arXiv, fetchable, and a reviewer will find it. What survives: (a) our target is end-to-end perplexity damage MAGNITUDE (R²=0.971, median error x1.01), theirs is a rank correlation against attention KL; (b) 26 models / 13 lineages / 176 cells vs 6 models under 7B on 128-token WikiText-103 windows; (c) their Fisher approximation self-admittedly breaks where ours must work. ACTION: cite HeadQ explicitly and prominently, concede the coordinate, and claim the calibration + scale + catastrophic regime. Do not let a reviewer discover it first.

2) HIGGS, arXiv:2411.17525 — SEVERE ON METHOD IDENTITY. The researcher's note says "its quadratic model is explicitly valid only above ~3 bits", which is right, but understates the overlap: HIGGS's calibration protocol is verbatim multi-magnitude Gaussian noise insertion with no quantizer ("we apply multiple (J) calibration noise levels that are uniformly sampled from applicability region"). That is our experimental design. A hostile reviewer's one-line summary is "HIGGS with a different error coordinate, applied to KV". The defense has to be three concrete numbers, not a framing: KV not weights; score-space not weight-L2; damage range x0.99–x1348 with zero dangerous misses versus a model that its own authors say diverges below 3 bits.

3) Alignment Collapse, arXiv:2606.09864 — SEVERE ON THE CHECKER/PRODUCT CLAIM. Eleven instruction-tuned models, five benchmarks, a cheap 20-prompt / 35-GPU-minute diagnostic (PCR) that predicts the failure mode on a held-out independent model family, and the explicit claim that KV quantization has "sharp model-specific phase transitions invisible to standard metrics". That is kvcheck.py's pitch, at larger model scale, six weeks old, revised ten days ago. Only the target variable differs (refusal/safety vs perplexity damage). If our paper's abstract says "cheap pre-deployment check for KV quantization", this paper eats it. Reframe around calibrated perplexity-damage PREDICTION, cite them as complementary, and consider actually running our score-space noise measure against their models to show it also predicts their alignment collapse — that would turn a collision into a strengthening result.

4) RateQuant, arXiv:2605.06675 — MODERATE-HIGH. It already fits distortion curves from a small calibration set and uses them to predict-and-allocate, and its "distortion model mismatch" finding stakes out the claim that distortion curves are quantizer-specific and non-transferable. If our per-model curve is described loosely as "fit a curve, predict damage", it collapses into RateQuant. The distinction is real and must be stated in the abstract: their curve is indexed by QUANTIZER and must be refit per quantizer; ours is indexed by MODEL and is evaluated at any quantizer's measured score-space noise. Also worth pre-empting: a reviewer will ask us to test the damage law on KIVI and QuaRot at 2.5 bits on Qwen3-8B, RateQuant's exact headline configuration.

5) Statistical Inference / Quality Measures Inspired by TurboQuant, arXiv:2605.08114 — MODERATE, AND UNCOMFORTABLY CLOSE TO HOME. An independent author is publishing quality measures for TurboQuant-family KV quantizers (WHT + MSE + QJL, exactly our stack), using KL divergence, and reaching the K/V asymmetry conclusion the project reached empirically. It does not collide on evidence — it is entirely synthetic, Beta-on-hypersphere, zero real-model per

**Open problems (author-stated).**

- HIGGS / Linearity Theorem (2411.17525), verbatim: 'the predicted perplexity is close to the real perplexity on relatively higher bitwidths (b>3.0) and diverges on lower bitwidths.' The authors bound their own model out of exactly the regime our damage law claims (2-bit and the x1348 catastrophic cells). This is the single most quotable opening in the whole slice — the incumbent method's authors say it stops working precisely where ours starts.
- HIGGS future work, verbatim: 'validating the approach across several model architecture types (e.g. Mixture-of-Experts)' remains unexplored; and 'the requirement to use Hadamard transforms for weight incoherence, which may add runtime overheads in some cases'. Our 26-model / 13-lineage atlas can answer the architecture-generality half directly, and our WHT-rotation cache is on the other side of the Hadamard-overhead question.
- HeadQ (2605.03562), verbatim limitations: 'The paper is mechanistic, not a serving-system claim'; 'We do not study packed kernels, latency, or throughput'; 'The model panel uses short real-corpus windows and six models up to Mistral 7B, below the 7B–70B frontier'; 'Query quantization also remains out of scope'. The model-panel admission is an explicit invitation for a larger atlas, and the serving-system admission is an invitation for a real bit-packed cache + checker.
- HeadQ's stated failure mode of the Fisher proxy: the quadratic approximation's 'explicit failure mode occurs when perturbations move probability mass between competing top tokens, at which point the quadratic approximation may not track exact KL reliably.' That failure mode IS the catastrophic regime. A law that stays calibrated through mass reallocation between top tokens is a documented open problem, stated by the closest prior work.
- Statistical Inference / TurboQuant quality measures (2605.08114), verbatim: the budget-dependent crossover where QKQV wins geometric K reconstruction at n in {2,3,5} and KQV at n in {4,6}, 'invariant to rank and tail weight -- an open rate-distortion problem.' Also implicitly open: the entire paper is synthetic (Beta on the hypersphere), so whether its KL/geometric conclusions hold on real model caches is untested — and that is exactly what a measured atlas answers.
- From Signal Degradation to Computation Collapse (2604.19884): screening by per-channel error rate flags risk 'without fully determining the failure multiplier'. The authors concede they can raise an alarm but cannot say how bad it will be. Predicting the multiplier is the stated gap.
- Alignment Collapse (2606.09864): 'no universal safe bit-width exists, with sharp model-specific phase transitions invisible to standard metrics', and perplexity 'remains near-baseline at bit-widths where alignment has already collapsed for most models, confirming that PPL is an unreliable proxy for safety behavior.' Open problem stated: locating the per-model phase transition cheaply, on axes perplexity does not see.
- Runtime-Certified Bounded-Error Quantized Attention (2605.20868), verbatim: 'The certification is local (per-head, per-step) and does not guarantee end-to-end model correctness.' Lifting a local per-step bound to an end-to-end damage guarantee is explicitly unsolved.
- Benchmarking KV-Cache Optimizations (2607.05399): 'compression ratio alone is a poor predictor of end-to-end performance', motivating 'workload-aware selection of KV-cache mechanisms rather than one-size-fits-all compression'. Open: no selection rule exists that takes a model and a quantizer and returns predicted damage — the paper only shows the need.
- Rethinking KV Cache Compression for LLM Serving (2503.24000, MLSys 2025): memory savings do not translate into throughput, compression can lengthen outputs and raise latency, and quality varies sharply by task. Open problem: the field's headline compression numbers are not the numbers that matter in serving.
- Predicting LLM Compression Degradation from Spectral Statistics (2604.18085): validated only on two model families and four low-rank WEIGHT methods; the 'predict-then-compress workflow' is proposed but not demonstrated for quantization or for KV cache at all.
- Perplexity Can Miss SAE Feature Damage (2606.03002): 'behavioral metrics alone are insufficient evidence that full-precision interpretability findings transfer to quantized models' — with INT7 improving perplexity while degrading 18.7% of active SAE features. Open: what the right ground-truth target for damage even is. This is a limitation of OUR target variable, not just theirs.
- Physics of KV Compression (2603.01426): the sharp hallucination cliff near 90% compression and its correlation with Global Eviction Ratio are demonstrated only on SYNTHETIC probing tasks, not real benchmarks — the authors' own framing is 'a structural probe of attention geometry', leaving real-workload validation open.
- KVPress (NVIDIA): stated that some presses 'depend on model architecture and might not work with all models', and coverage is tested mainly on Llama/Mistral/Phi3/Qwen/Gemma. There is no standardized harness that evaluates KV QUANTIZERS with cheap proxies and ground truth on the same forward pass — the community infrastructure is eviction-first.

### Low-rank / SVD-based KV-cache compression (the "compress the hidden dimension" family, as opposed to quantization or eviction), and specifically the head-to-head question: how does low-rank compare to

**State of the art.** Two numbers define this territory, and they point in opposite directions — which is the exploitable tension.

(1) THE HEAD-TO-HEAD VERDICT. arXiv:2604.11501 (Apr 2026) is the only paper that runs the equal-memory comparison properly, and it says quantization wins decisively: at matched storage across five models (124M-14B, MHA and GQA), quantization beats rank reduction by 4-364 PPL. The defining datapoint is joint K+V INT4 = 75% total KV reduction for +0.18 PPL on Mistral 7B, versus rank-32 at identical storage collapsing LAMBADA accuracy to 0.4% and reaching 377.00 PPL on Qwen 3B. The theoretical statement is that projection damage exceeds quantization damage by 3*2^(2b) per direction under the softmax Fisher metric — 768x at INT4 — because deleting a dimension can flip which token wins the softmax, a discrete failure that bounded noise avoids. A basis ablation (spread <0.4 PPL) shows this is basis-independent, so no cleverer subspace rescues rank reduction. The gap GROWS with GQA aggressiveness, i.e. it gets worse on exactly the modern architectures that matter.

(2) THE BEST LOW-RANK NUMBERS. Pure low-rank tops out well below quantization's ratios: Eigen Attention 40%, Palu 50%, ReCalKV/JoLT 2-3x near-lossless, STAR-KV 75% (4x) at 7.34 vs 6.86 PPL, xKV 8x cross-layer (the outlier high-water mark), SALS 6.4x. Stacked with quantization the numbers get competitive: STAR-KV reports 20x total at 3.2-bit with 59.18% average accuracy. But read that table honestly and it reinforces (1): KIVI, a plain 3.16-bit quantizer at only 5.1x, still scores HIGHER average accuracy (60.3%) than STAR-KV's 20x hybrid. Nobody in this slice shows pure low-rank beating pure quantization on accuracy at equal memory.

(3) THE CONTRADICTION. Palu (ICLR 2025) explicitly claims it 'maintains comparable or even better accuracy (up to 1.19 lower perplexity) compared to quantization-only methods.' That is flatly inconsistent with 2604.11501's headline, and Eigen Attention reports a CROSSOVER (quantized standard attention wins at large KV budgets; quantized Eigen Attention wins as precision drops). Nobody has adjudicated this. The most likely resolution is memory accounting: low-rank papers frequently do not charge themselves for reconstruction matrices, per-head bases, and full-rank anchor tokens, while quantization papers do not always charge for scales and codebooks — the same class of accounting error that turned this researcher's own cache from a claimed win into a measured 0.17x E

**Collision risk.** RANKED BY SEVERITY. Three of these are serious enough to change how the paper is framed.

=== SEVERITY 1: arXiv:2604.11501, "Quantization Dominates Rank Reduction for KV-Cache Compression" (13 Apr 2026) ===
This paper takes three flags at once.
(a) SCORE SPACE AS THE COORDINATE. It measures compression damage in the softmax Fisher metric G = diag(alpha) - alpha*alpha^T — that IS score space. Any claim of the form "we contribute the score-space coordinate" is dead on arrival. The researcher's own brief already concedes HeadQ (withdrawn) published score-space-as-correct-coordinate; 2604.11501 is the un-withdrawn version, and KQ-SVD (2512.05916) proves a related optimality statement about the attention matrix from the low-rank side. That is three independent publications. Score space is now background.
(b) SCORE-SPACE PROXY PREDICTS PERPLEXITY DAMAGE. It publishes "Attention KL is monotonically correlated with PPL (r=0.99)" with Figure 3 captioned "Attention routing disruption (KL divergence) predicts compression damage." This is the one-model shadow of the damage law.
(c) THE EQUAL-MEMORY VERDICT ITSELF. "Quantization beats low-rank at matched storage" is now published with numbers. Do not plan to publish that as a finding.
WHAT SURVIVES: their r=0.99 covers 3 methods at ONE budget (512-bit) on ONE model (Mistral 7B, per-sequence, 3 seeds). They inject NO noise without a quantizer, fit NO calibration curve, make NO cross-model claim, predict NO unseen configuration, and define NO safety gate. They state in print: "Our theory provides an explanation for the observed results, not a universal law." The researcher's 96 configurations / 13 models / R^2 0.971 / x0.99-to-x1348 damage range / held-out 5-6-8-bit within x1.5 / zero dangerous misses at a x5 gate is a categorically different artifact. REQUIRED REFRAME: the contribution is not "score space is the right coordinate" but "a per-model noise-response curve, calibrated with a quantizer-free perturbation, transfers to unseen quantizer configurations and gates catastrophe." Mitigating factors: it is a solo-author, 16-page, unreviewed preprint with no listed venue and no code found; the researcher's 13-model evidence base is far broader. Aggravating factor: it is four months old and directly on-topic, so reviewers will know it.

=== SEVERITY 2: KV-CoRE, arXiv:2602.05929 (5 Feb 2026) ===
Collides with the SHAPE and the CLAIM of asset #2. They already ran a cross-model, cross-dataset benchmark (7 models, 20 datasets across 5 English domains and 16 languages) pairing a cheap spectral proxy against measured Normalized Delta-Perplexity, and they explicitly claim "the first large-scale benchmark of kv-cache compressibility in LLMs" and that NER is "a reliable predictor of performance sensitivity." The phrase "first large-scale KV compressibility atlas" is taken. WHAT SURVIVES: their proxy is spectral (effective rank) not score-space; they build NO predictive model (correlation only, and they say so); their key-side correlation is weak (r=0.64); they cover only SVD truncation with zero quantizer configurations; they report no damage magnitudes, no catastrophic regime, no held-out generalization, and no limitations section. Reframe asset #2 from "first atlas" to "first atlas that pairs proxy metrics with END-TO-END damage across BOTH compression families and yields a predictive law rather than a correlation."

=== SEVERITY 2: JoLT, arXiv:2607.12550 (14 Jul 2026) ===
Collides with the ARCHITECTURE of asset #4. "A random orthogonal rotation followed by low-bit quantization" applied to a residual, with rank and bit-width jointly allocated under one byte budget, is the same design pattern as WHT rotation + Lloyd-Max + residual signs. Published five weeks ago. Combined with TurboQuant/QJL on the quantization side, rotation-plus-residual is now prior art as a KV cache design. Do not claim it as novel. WHAT SURVIVES: JoLT only reaches 2-3x near-lossless versus the researcher's measured 5.54x, uses 

**Open problems (author-stated).**

- arXiv:2604.11501, verbatim Limitations: 'Five models up to 14B; larger models untested. LAMBADA on two models. No wall-clock latency (rank reduction saves attention compute; quantization saves bandwidth). Cache-level quantization validated at INT8/INT4 only.' — Every one of these is inside a solo 4090's reach except the largest models. In particular 'cache-level quantization validated at INT8/INT4 only' is an explicit gap at exactly the 3-bit sweet spot and the 2-bit catastrophic edge where the researcher's 96-configuration study lives, and the researcher already has held-out 5/6/8-bit results the paper lacks.
- arXiv:2604.11501, verbatim Discussion caveat: 'We do not claim this extends to all compression settings, all models, or all tasks. Our theory provides an explanation for the observed results, not a universal law.' — The author is explicitly declining to claim a law. That is an open invitation, in print, for exactly the artifact the researcher already has: a calibrated, cross-model, held-out-validated damage law. Their r=0.99 attention-KL-to-PPL result is validated on 3 methods at ONE budget on ONE model (Mistral 7B, 3 seeds), which they state plainly.
- arXiv:2604.11501's Proposition 2 asserts projection damage and quantization damage differ by a factor 3*2^(2b) PER DIRECTION under the softmax Fisher metric, i.e. low-rank damage is anisotropic and rank-deficient rather than isotropic. Nobody has tested whether a damage law calibrated with mean-free ISOTROPIC Gaussian noise still predicts LOW-RANK configurations. This is the single sharpest open problem in the slice and it is a one-GPU experiment.
- OjaKV, verbatim Limitations: 'OjaKV introduces fixed hyperparameters (learning rates, buffer size, top-k) that may require tuning for different models or tasks. While the Oja update is lightweight, it still incurs additional computational overhead compared to static compression methods. Third, our hybrid selection strategy relies on reconstruction error, which may not perfectly capture token importance for all downstream tasks.' — The third clause is the researcher's atlas finding, admitted by the authors themselves.
- OjaKV, verbatim Future Work: 'A promising direction is to replace fixed update schedules with adaptive ones... decode-stage reconstruction error provides a natural signal for when the current low-rank subspace is no longer well aligned with the evolving context.' — They propose reconstruction error as the trigger signal; the researcher has direct evidence that reconstruction-space proxies are uncorrelated with real damage (Pearson -0.003), so substituting a score-space trigger is a targeted, authors-invited improvement.
- OjaKV admits an unresolved cost: TTFT at 32K rises from 2102 ms (full KV) to 2801 ms, plus 10.9-13.4% decode latency overhead, and the paper does NOT compare against KV quantization at equivalent memory budgets at all — a gap the authors leave open.
- Eigen Attention, verbatim Limitation: 'we do not extensively study the best dataset for basis generation.' All results use 512 WikiText samples; robustness across domains is untested. Their Future Work asks for 'finding the best combination of various compression techniques... to achieve extreme KV cache compression' — i.e. the hybrid allocation problem is explicitly left open by the paper that first framed low-rank attention.
- Eigen Attention reports its own unresolved crossover: 'at large KV cache size, the quantized standard attention outperforms quantized Eigen Attention... as quantization precision is reduced... quantized Eigen Attention outperforms quantized standard attention.' Where that crossover sits, per model, is unquantified and unexplained anywhere in the literature.
- STAR-KV (ICML 2026 spotlight) publishes NO Limitations section and NO future work, and does NOT compare low-rank against quantization-only methods at equal memory — its own paper says it evaluates combined approaches instead. Its 20x result also requires a calibration fine-tune on 3000 FineWeb-Edu samples (<6 GPU hours), so it is not training-free.
- KV-CoRE publishes NO Limitations section and NO future work. Its own numbers concede the key half of the problem is unsolved: NER predicts value-cache degradation at r=0.88 but KEY-cache degradation at only r=0.64. Keys are the harder and more damage-critical side, and their proxy is visibly weak there. They gesture at 'dynamic, data-aware compression strategies' as an implication rather than committing to it.
- CARE's own diagnosis of the field: weight-only low-rank baselines 'focus on minimizing the difference between weight matrices rather than on how those weights affect input activations, ignore the covariance structure of activations, and enforce uniform rank across layers, causing activation drift and degraded attention fidelity.' CARE itself still needs a post-SVD healing fine-tune to fully recover accuracy — so training-free MLA conversion at matched KV width remains unsolved.
- SALS states a structural obstacle nobody has removed: RoPE applied to keys increases their variance and therefore their rank, and low-rank caches must be reconstructed before RoPE can be applied, 'creating a new speed bottleneck.' Low-rank fights the position encoding that every modern model uses; quantization does not.
- JoLT allocates Tucker ranks and residual bit-widths under one byte budget by an objective validated with relative FROBENIUS error (0.009 K, 0.006 V at 2x) and only reaches 2-3x near-lossless. Whether a damage-aware allocation objective beats a Frobenius-optimal one at the same byte budget is untested by them.
- arXiv:2603.01426 reports 'a sharp hallucination safety cliff near 90% compression' as an empirical phase transition with no predictive model, and observes that 'moderate compression degrades internal representations with little accuracy loss' — i.e. standard benchmarks hide damage that is already present. It leaves both the location of the cliff and the early-warning threshold unmodelled.

**Key works.**

### Newest KV-cache quantization METHODS of 2026 (arXiv 26xx), focused on what is SOTA at 2-bit and 3-bit KV today, on which benchmarks, with which numbers. Verified via arXiv abs-page fetches (~24 fetche

**State of the art.** TWO-BIT (the active frontier). SOTA is a rotation/transform-coding family evaluated on reasoning + long-context retrieval, NOT perplexity. The chain in 2026: QuaRot-INT2 (collapses: 0.0-17.3 on most cells) -> OSCAR (2605.17757, May 2026, ~2.28 bits/element) -> beaten in August by two independent papers. The defining numbers today: (a) NOVA-KV (2608.04074) at 2.22 bits — RULER-NIAH 128K on Qwen3-8B 75.4 vs BF16 83.4, where OSCAR gets 25.3 and QuaRot 0.0; and on GPT-OSS-20B at 2.41 bits, 54.0 NIAH and 72.4 reasoning-mean vs BF16 76.5, where OSCAR collapses to 0.0/13.9. (b) OptR (2608.02691), which is a composable correction rather than a standalone quantizer: it lifts QuaRot-INT2 on Qwen3-8B AIME25 from 17.33 to 66.67 and OSCAR from 54.67 to 66.00, against BF16 68.00. (c) KVarN (2606.03458) claims 2-bit SOTA on generative benchmarks (Qwen3-4B AIME24 60.0, MATH500 79.2, HumanEval 88.4; Phi-4-14B MATH500 84.8) and is the only one shipped as a native vLLM backend — but its repo headline config is k4v2, so its 2-bit claim needs checking. The practical summary: at ~2.2-2.3 bits per element, a well-designed attention-aware transform now lands within 1.5-4 points of BF16 on 4B-32B reasoning models and stays usable at 128K, and it is EXPECTED to ship as a paged-cache-compatible INT2 kernel in vLLM/SGLang. Scalar per-channel/per-token methods (KIVI, plain QuaRot) are no longer competitive at 2 bits on reasoning benchmarks.

THREE-BIT (largely considered solved, and the goalpost has moved past it). TurboQuant (2504.19874) set the reference — quality-neutral at 3.5 bits, marginal degradation at 2.5. That reference has been beaten twice in 2026 at LOWER bit-widths: eOptShrinkQ (2605.02905) scores 47.4/48.3 on LongBench-16 at 2.22 bits versus TurboQuant-prod's 45.2/46.6 at 3.00 bits on Llama-3.1-8B and Ministral-8B, i.e. roughly 0.78 bits saved at better quality; and AATC (2608.14191) reports near-lossless accuracy at ~5.8x compression across LongBench, RULER, GSM8K, MMLU-Pro and MATH-500 on Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct and Qwen-2.5-7B-Instruct. Meanwhile Block-GTQ (2606.24033) showed that UNIFORM TurboQuant-MSE at 3-bit keys / 2-bit values scores 0.0/0.0 on AIME24/25 with DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B where its RoPE-aware reallocation of the same average budget scores 51.7/37.5 against fp16's 54.2/37.9. So the honest statement is: 3-bit KV is near-lossless ONLY with a non-uniform allocation or a shared-structure removal in front of it; uniform 3-bit is a known catastrophic-failu

**Collision risk.** Ranked most to least dangerous. Two of these are serious enough to change how the paper is framed.

1. SEVERE — Alignment Collapse / PCR (arXiv:2606.09864, June 2026, v2 August 2026). This is the same paper SHAPE as assets 1+2+4 and the researcher already cites it, almost certainly under-weighted. Overlap: eleven models (3.8B-72B) vs thirteen; a cheap per-model diagnostic (20 prompts, ~35 GPU-minutes) vs 5-11 noise-injection forward passes; the explicit headline that 'no universal safe bit-width exists, with sharp model-specific phase transitions INVISIBLE TO STANDARD METRICS' — which is the researcher's atlas headline restated; held-out generalization to an unseen model family, matching the held-out claim; and generalization across 'unseen prompts, models, and production quantizers'. It even reports the researcher's central irony independently (Mistral-7B loses 15.2% of refusals at only 1.03x perplexity, i.e. a proxy that looks fine while the model is broken). WHAT SURVIVES: PCR outputs a 3-way CLASSIFICATION of failure mode plus a mitigation direction; the damage law outputs a CONTINUOUS, CALIBRATED MAGNITUDE (R^2 0.971, x0.99 to x1348, median error x1.01, zero dangerous misses at a x5 gate). PCR's outcome variable is refusal rate, not perplexity; its coordinate is a per-channel activation subspace, not score space; it prescribes fixes rather than predicting damage at an arbitrary configuration. ACTION: the paper must cite this in the CONTRIBUTION paragraph, not only in related work, and must state the difference as classification-vs-regression and alignment-vs-perplexity. Do not claim novelty for 'cheap per-model calibration predicts model-specific KV failure invisible to standard metrics' — that sentence is taken.

2. SEVERE — Block-GTQ / RoPE-Aware Bit Allocation (arXiv:2606.24033, June 2026, code live). Independently occupies four things the researcher believes are distinctive: (a) a multi-model panel built around TurboQuant-family quantizers, explicitly described as a 'ten-model diagnostic panel' selected for 'architectural coverage rather than leaderboard coverage' — the atlas's exact design rationale; (b) a LOGIT-SPACE fidelity metric (per-layer RoPE query-key logit MAE) as the primary diagnostic, with 367/367 layer comparisons — i.e. score-space measurement at scale; (c) a LABEL-FREE cheap statistic (AM-GM energy score from marginal Q/K second moments, no paired query-key products, robust to short calibration windows) — the same design goal as the researcher's cheap proxies; (d) the published observation that uniform TurboQuant-MSE COLLAPSES to 0.0/0.0 on AIME with a reasoning model. Its panel includes Qwen2.5-3B and Qwen2.5-14B. WHAT SURVIVES: they attribute collapse to RoPE frequency-block energy imbalance and fix it by bit allocation; they never measure a per-head shared key mean, never diagnose a family lineage, and never predict damage magnitude — their metric is a fidelity score used for allocation, not a calibrated predictor of perplexity damage. But 'TurboQuant catastrophically fails and logit space is where you see it, measured across a ten-model architectural panel' is now published by someone else. ACTION: read this paper in full before writing anything, and check whether their per-layer MAE profile on Qwen2.5-3B reproduces the researcher's layer-0/layer-27 signature — if it does, that is a strong independent corroboration to cite; if it does not, it is evidence the two mechanisms are distinct.

3. HIGH, on the mechanism asset — OptR (arXiv:2608.02691, August 2026) contains 'an attention-equivalent key reparameterization to reduce large channel-wise offsets without changing the softmax distribution'. That is the researcher's mean-removal, stated with the same justification (the removed component is attention-inert), inside an August 2026 SOTA method. Combined with InnerQ (2602.23200, Feb 2026) which folds 'per-channel normalization of the key cache' into the model parameters at zero runtime cost, and NSNQu

**Open problems (author-stated).**

- Block-GTQ (2606.24033), verbatim from its limitations: 'Block-GTQ allocates bits only on K, leaving V uniform. A V-side allocator, joint K+V optimization, and denser packing could further reduce memory. The fused decode path is an initial single-GPU implementation; multi-GPU and batched serving are open directions.' Value-side allocation is unclaimed in the RoPE-aware line.
- NOVA-KV (2608.04074), verbatim: 'Joint quantization of keys and values is left for future work' — they vector-quantize keys but scalar-quantize values. Also: 'The high-resolution model might not hold at 2 bits, we use the theory to guide our design rather than as a guarantee.' The SOTA 2-bit method openly admits its own theory is out of validity range at the bit-width it is deployed at. That is precisely the gap an EMPIRICAL per-model calibration curve fills.
- KVarN (2606.03458), Appendix E, verbatim: 'Some recent models use train-time compression for the KV-Cache in the form of multi-head latent attention (MLA). As in other KV-Cache compression methods, it is unclear how such attention mechanisms affect quantization quality.' MLA is a stated blind spot across the field, and Block-GTQ's panel contains exactly one MLA model (DeepSeek-V2-Lite).
- KVarN (2606.03458), Appendix E, verbatim: 'End-to-end evaluation on a publicly available serving framework is hindered by the fact that currently such frameworks do not support 2-bit KV-Caches.' The 2-bit SOTA is measured largely in research harnesses, not production serving stacks — so published 2-bit numbers are not yet independently reproducible end-to-end.
- eOptShrinkQ (2605.02905), Section 8, verbatim: 'Online SVD updates that incrementally maintain the rank estimate and shrunken factors as tokens arrive would eliminate this buffering requirement, but adapting the BBP phase transition test and noise spectral estimation to the streaming setting remains an open problem.' The current method must buffer 128 tokens and keeps the most recent <=127 tokens in FP16. Streaming shared-structure removal is explicitly open.
- eOptShrinkQ (2605.02905), Remark A.3: the theoretical guarantees assume 'signal singular vectors as random', which the authors concede 'may not fully hold at inference time when attention patterns become deterministic.' Its future work names 'extending to larger models (70B+), integrating with other KV cache reduction techniques (token eviction, cross-layer sharing), and developing hardware-aware implementations with latency benchmarks.'
- OSCAR (2605.17757), Appendix D, verbatim: 'OSCAR's calibration requires a small dataset of model activations, which adds a one-time overhead' — and it relies on a frozen-error diagonal residual assumption that 'may not hold universally across all architectures or quantization regimes.' They also note that 'at extreme sparsity or highly irregular attention patterns, the covariance-based approach may be less effective,' and say this is not extensively validated. Architecture-dependence of a fixed calibrated rotation is an admitted, unmeasured risk.
- OptR (2608.02691) has NO explicit limitations section, but its discussion concedes three: offline calibration of 6-11 minutes per domain on an A100 limits deployment to new domains; performance still degrades at 128K contexts; and the lambda_K balance parameter 'depends on the rotation initialization', so the method is not initialization-agnostic.
- DGAP (2607.16248), Section 7, verbatim: 'DGAP focuses on algorithmic distribution restoration at decode time, while leaving system-level serving optimizations as future work.' It also depends on 'offline-calibrated thresholds and recovery windows derived from paired FP16 and quantized traces' — i.e. it needs the FP16 reference it is trying to replace — and names 'online calibration and adaptive window selection for deployment-specific workloads' as open.
- DGAP (2607.16248) states a direct scientific disagreement worth exploiting: it argues the root cause of low-bit failure is 'structured local misranking, where the distribution of logits in top-K region is drifted', explicitly 'rather than common beliefs that absolute error of logits'. Whether a logit-CORRELATION scalar or a top-K RANK-drift statistic is the better damage coordinate is an open, decidable question that nobody has settled.
- IsoQuant (2603.28430) admits its 'validation [is] limited to stage-1 quantization path on synthetic vectors; full end-to-end KV-cache evaluation deferred.' A published rotation method in this space has no end-to-end model evaluation at all.
- Alignment Collapse (2606.09864) establishes as a finding-and-open-problem that 'no universal safe bit-width exists, with sharp model-specific phase transitions invisible to standard metrics', and that safety features live in a subspace 10^2-10^3x more noise-vulnerable than what perplexity averages over — meaning perplexity-calibrated damage predictions may be systematically non-conservative for alignment-relevant behavior. Nobody has connected a perplexity-space damage law to an alignment-space one.
- Benchmarking KV-Cache Optimizations (2607.05399) states the field-level problem plainly: KV-cache compression techniques 'are difficult to compare because they were evaluated on different models, tasks, budgets, and serving stacks', and finds 'the compression ratio alone is a poor predictor of end-to-end performance'. The absence of a common cheap predictor across methods and models is an acknowledged gap.
- AATC (2608.14191): its limitations could NOT be verified — the arXiv HTML returns 404 and a PDF summarization returned demonstrably hallucinated content. Treat any claim about this paper beyond its abstract as unverified until the PDF is read directly. This is itself an action item, because it is the paper whose theoretical framing sits closest to the damage law.

### Theory and practice of predicting perplexity/loss increase from quantization error — the "damage law" literature. Three distinct lineages exist: (1) WEIGHT-side noise-insertion calibration with a quad

**State of the art.** There are three separate "best" answers because the territory is not unified, and the researcher's asset sits in the seam between them.

FOR WEIGHTS, the state of the art is HIGGS's noise-insertion-calibrated linear model: PPL ~= PPL* + sum_l alpha_l * t_l^2, with alpha_l fitted per layer from J=15 mean-free isotropic Gaussian noise insertions and NO quantizer, calibrated on 287k WikiText-2 tokens. The defining number is the validity boundary, stated verbatim: predictions track measured perplexity for b > 3.0 and diverge below. Kumar et al.'s precision-aware scaling laws are the aggregate-loss counterpart (465+ pretraining runs, validated to 1.7B params / 26B tokens), and QiD (1500+ checkpoints) the population-regression counterpart.

FOR THE KV CACHE, there is NO calibrated end-to-end damage law at all. The best available substitutes are: (a) RateQuant's allocation objective sum_i w_i * alpha * beta^{-b_i} with gradient-based per-head sensitivity — defining number KIVI at 2.5 average bits on Qwen3-8B, PPL 49.3 -> 14.9, calibration 1.6 s, and a fitted TurboQuant Lloyd-Max curve of alpha ~= 1.36, beta ~= 3.48 at R^2 > 0.99; (b) HeadQ's local Fisher/score-space ranking proxy — defining number ~0.62-0.69 Spearman points over raw key MSE at ranking attention KL on six models, but that paper is withdrawn; (c) the single reported attention-KL-to-PPL correlation of r = 0.99, from one model at one matched budget in arXiv:2604.11501. For a forward-only layer-sensitivity proxy the honest bar is Kendall tau 0.79 (KL) versus 0.76 (SQNR) against per-layer delta-PPL.

FOR THE CATASTROPHIC REGIME, the closest thing to a predictive law is 12 days old: margin shrinkage, m' = c*m + b + eps, with c collapsing at median 0.86 / 0.33 / 0.00 at 4 / 3 / 2 bits, beating four additive accounts in 539 of 633 damaged cells and predicting held-out decision-flip rates to median 1.7 points from a condition's own constants. Its stated failure is transfer: borrowed constants are off by 18-33 points at 3 bits. Everything else in the catastrophic regime is qualitative (two failure modes: Signal Degradation vs Computation Collapse; non-linear breakpoints; phase transitions with no universal safe bit-width).

So the number the researcher's asset must be defended against is not one number but a claim shape: nobody has published a cardinal, out-of-sample, cross-model damage prediction for the KV cache spanning the benign and catastrophic regimes with a single curve. R^2 0.971 over 96 configurat

**Collision risk.** Ranked by how much each would cost if a reviewer found it first. Nothing here sinks the work, but two items require the claim to be re-scoped before submission, and one requires an experiment.

TIER 1 — RE-SCOPE REQUIRED.

(1) HIGGS / arXiv:2411.17525 owns noise-insertion calibration outright. Mean-free isotropic Gaussian perturbation at multiple magnitudes, no quantizer, regress damage, then evaluate the fitted curve at a real quantizer's measured error — that is Appendix B.2 plus Algorithm 3, published 21 months ago, at J=15 noise levels on 287k WikiText-2 tokens. The METHOD is not novel and must not be presented as such. What survives cleanly: the coordinate (score-space, not relative Frobenius weight error), the object (KV cache, not weights), the regime (below their stated b > 3.0 boundary), and the functional form (one per-model curve at a worst-layer statistic, versus their additive sum of per-layer alpha_l t_l^2 — a real difference that QEP's error-compounding result justifies). Frame the contribution as "HIGGS's calibration idea, moved to the coordinate and regime where HIGGS says it fails."

(2) arXiv:2608.06564 (6-10 Aug 2026) landed "damage is multiplicative, not additive" twelve days before this survey, with 16 models across 8 families, a fitted law, and held-out prediction. It also landed "constants must be measured per model". The separations are genuine — weights not KV, decision-margin flips not perplexity, and its own transfer result is negative where the researcher's is positive — but the TRANSFER CLAIM MUST BE STATED PRECISELY. Their negative result is transfer of constants ACROSS MODELS; the researcher's positive result is transfer to unseen BIT-WIDTHS WITHIN a model's own curve. Those are different claims and the writeup must say so in the same breath, or this paper becomes the rebuttal. Concrete risk: if cross-model transfer of the noise curve has never been tested, a reviewer will assume it fails as it does here. Test it and report it either way.

TIER 2 — CITE, DIFFERENTIATE, DO NOT IGNORE.

(3) HeadQ / arXiv:2605.03562 is the score-space coordinate for KV, with six-model evidence that it beats key MSE, AND a softmax-null mean-key intervention that overlaps the shared-key-mean mechanism in asset #3. It is withdrawn for ethical concerns, which makes it a citation problem rather than a clean priority loss — but it is in the permanent arXiv record with v2 HTML still served, so assume it surfaces. Differentiators are strong and should be explicit: their proxy is validated against attention KL, never end-to-end perplexity; they disclaim global validity precisely in the route-flip regime; their mechanism is one family-specific boundary in small Pythia models, not a five-generation lineage diagnosis decomposed into k_proj bias plus massive activations and reproduced on demand.

(4) arXiv:2604.11501 reports attention KL monotonically correlated with PPL at r = 0.99. This is the "already known" objection in its most quotable form. The answer is quantitative and available: one model / one matched budget / three methods / ordinal, versus 96 configurations / 13 models / cardinal / out-of-sample / damage spanning x0.99 to x1348. Say it in those terms.

(5) arXiv:2606.09864 (alignment collapse) is the closest existing multi-model KV atlas with a cheap per-model diagnostic (PCR, 20 calibration prompts, ~35 GPU-minutes) that predicts a mitigation and generalizes to a held-out family — structurally the same instrument shape as kvcheck.py plus the noise curves, over 11 models that overlap the 26. It is also the sharpest threat to the OUTCOME VARIABLE: 15.2% of refusals lost at 1.03x perplexity is a damage the x5 catastrophe gate scores as safe. "Zero dangerous misses" must be scoped explicitly to perplexity damage.

(6) arXiv:2606.24033 (Block-GTQ) already runs a ten-model diagnostic panel in logit space, built on TurboQuant-MSE, winning 367/367 layer comparisons. The framing "logit-space diagnostic across N models" is 

**Open problems (author-stated).**

- HIGGS never quantifies its own applicability region, and left the TODO in the published paper. Appendix C states the model holds 'for all t_l in [0, t_bar_l], where t_bar_l is "small enough"' with a footnote whose entire text is: 'Add some comment on how small it is.' The empirical boundary is given only as an observation: 'We observe that the predicted perplexity is close to the real perplexity on relatively higher bitwidths (b>3.0) and diverges on lower bitwidths, where the quantization error is higher. Thus, we can use the error model in realistic bitwidth ranges.' A closed-form or measured characterization of t_bar_l is an open problem the authors flagged and did not close — and a damage law that stays accurate BELOW that boundary answers a question the field's most-cited paper on this topic explicitly left blank.
- HIGGS's own Limitations section covers only two items, neither of which is the validity regime: 'One direction for improvement is validating the approach across several model architecture types (e.g. Mixture-of-Experts). However, we believe our result should be generalizable, as the quantization approach used is model-independent. One other limitation is the requirement to use Hadamard transforms for weight incoherence, which may add runtime overheads in some cases.' The unaddressed cross-architecture validation is precisely what a 26-model / 13-lineage atlas supplies.
- HeadQ concedes its score-space proxy is local-only and names its failure mode: 'We therefore use Eq. 4 as a local diagnostic and ranking proxy for attention damage, not as a global equality for downstream loss. Its explicit failure mode is finite route flipping: once a perturbation moves probability mass between competing top tokens, the quadratic need not track exact KL reliably.' And in Section 8: 'The Fisher/KL proxy is likewise local by design. The matched Pythia route-flip slice is the paper's explicit counterexample to global validity.' The route-flip regime is the catastrophic regime — so the paper that owns the score-space coordinate disclaims it exactly where the researcher's law claims to work.
- HeadQ also concedes scale and statistical weakness: 'The model panel uses short real-corpus windows and six models up to Mistral 7B, below the 7B-70B frontier of recent systems papers. The six-model KL-ranking table is a point-estimate summary rather than a confidence-interval result, and not every auxiliary probe spans exactly the same model set. The matched Pythia probes explain one verified family-specific route-flip boundary rather than a universal scaling law.' Both the 26-model atlas and the five-generation Qwen mechanism study are direct answers to a limitation this paper states in its own words.
- arXiv:2604.11501 volunteers that its softmax-routing account is not a general law: 'We do not claim this extends to all compression settings, all models, or all tasks. Our theory provides an explanation for the observed results, not a universal law.' Its r = 0.99 attention-KL-to-PPL correlation is therefore offered as an observation at one matched budget on one model, not as a predictive instrument — which is the gap between a correlation and a calibrated curve.
- The signal-noise SNR paper fences its scope to weights explicitly: 'Our analysis focuses on weight-only post-training quantization, allowing weight-induced errors to be isolated and compared systematically across bit-widths, quantization methods, tasks and model scales. The quantitative findings should be interpreted within this scope, rather than directly extrapolated to activation quantization.' The KV cache is activation-side. The authors are saying, ten days ago, that their result does not transfer to the researcher's setting.
- The margin-shrinkage paper states that its constants do not transfer across models and that this is unresolved: 'Borrowed constants are wrong by 18-33 points at 3 bits, so the paired margin set has to be measured per model and bit-width.' And on the held-out figure: 'At 4 bits it transfers (median error 0.5pp); below that it does not. The shape holds, the constants do not transfer.' It further lists as open: 'why large models preserve a larger fraction of their margin is open', 'The attention-gap measure that tracks the collapse within a model does not predict which models are more robust than others', and 'a synthetic layer stack built to reproduce the collapse predicts a scaling with network depth that real models violate'. Predicting WHICH models are fragile from a cheap forward-only measurement is stated as unsolved by the most recent paper in the territory — and rho (mean-dominance) measurements on 16 models plus 22 noise curves are a direct attempt at exactly that.
- The same paper disclaims causal grounding: 'We measure quantities alongside the collapse and rule out one hypothesis; no intervention establishes a causal path from a perturbed weight to a moved margin, so section 6 constrains an explanation rather than supplying one.' It also permanently withdrew one result from an earlier draft: 'the claim that a directly measured disturbance predicts cross-model robustness better than the constant derived from the amplification'. A causally verified mechanism — reproducing collapse on demand in an immune model by injecting a synthetic shared component — is the intervention this literature says it lacks.
- RateQuant's Appendix O names evaluation scope and design gaps: 'While the framework is model-agnostic by design, validation on additional architectures (Mistral, Gemma, DeepSeek) and long-context benchmarks (RULER, LongBench) would further demonstrate generality.' And: 'The current implementation allocates bits per head uniformly across all token positions. Position-aware allocation... is a natural extension... Similarly, the per-head independence assumption may be relaxed in future work to model correlated heads.' Notably it does NOT list its most consequential gap: its objective is weighted distortion, never end-to-end perplexity, so a user cannot ask it what a given allocation will cost.
- The TurboQuant statistical-inference paper poses a precisely-scoped open theory question the researcher's measured data can attack: 'What remains open is the quantitative derivation: under what condition does adding a 1-bit QJL residual corrector to (n-1)-bit scalar quantisation produce lower K direction error than n-bit scalar quantisation alone? A complete theory would derive the crossover budget n* from the rate-distortion curve of the Beta codebook and the JL residual variance as a function of n and d - a calculation we leave for future work.' The researcher has the Lloyd-Max codebooks, residual-sign machinery, and measurement harness to answer this, and RateQuant has already published the fitted Beta-codebook rate-distortion constants (alpha ~= 1.36, beta ~= 3.48) needed as input.
- QEP's Limitations are calibration-bound and hand-tuned: 'QEP relies on a small calibration set, as in other layer-wise PTQ approaches, which makes performance sensitive to data quality; however, it overfits less than comparable methods such as GPTQ and AWQ. The method also introduces a per-layer propagation-strength parameter alpha_l; Although this parameter is tunable, a fixed value, e.g. alpha_l = 1/2, works well in most cases, and automatic learning of alpha_l is left for future work.' The cross-layer amplification factor is a free parameter set by hand — measured per-model noise-response curves are a principled way to fit it from data.
- Three independent 2026 papers now argue perplexity itself is an inadequate damage measure, and none offers a replacement that is both cheap and end-to-end. Alignment collapse: Mistral-7B loses 15.2% of refusals at only 1.03x perplexity, with 'no universal safe bit-width' and 'sharp model-specific phase transitions invisible to standard metrics'. SAE feature damage: 'perplexity can miss feature damage: on Gemma-2-2B, INT7 improves perplexity while degrading 18.7% of active SAE features'. Illusion of equivalency: behavioral divergence 'emerges under moderate quantization even when task performance appears preserved'. Extending a calibrated damage law to a second, safety- or feature-level outcome variable measurable from the same forward pass is an open problem all three papers gesture at and none solves.
- Runtime-Certified Bounded-Error Quantized Attention states the composition gap outright in its abstract: 'The certification is local (per-head, per-step) and does not guarantee end-to-end model correctness, but ensures that each attention computation is either bounded relative to an FP16 reference or exactly recovered via fallback.' Turning a local per-head attention-distortion bound into a calibrated end-to-end damage forecast is the missing link, and it is named as missing by the paper that built the local bound.
- KVarN raises an evaluation-regime problem nobody in the damage-prediction literature has addressed: 'current methods are evaluated under prefill-like settings and errors behave differently under autoregressive decoding... in the latter regime, quantization errors accumulate across timesteps, driven primarily by incorrect token scales.' Every damage law surveyed here, including HIGGS's, is calibrated on teacher-forced perplexity. Whether calibrated curves under-predict damage on generative workloads is open, and it is the failure direction that would produce a dangerous miss.
