### KV-cache compression that is NOT quantization — specifically the systems half: offloading to CPU DRAM / NVMe / CXL, paged and virtual-memory KV management, prefix caching and cross-request cache reuse

**State of the art.** Two answers, because the slice has a systems SOTA and an economics SOTA.

SYSTEMS SOTA. The stack that defines the territory today is: PagedAttention/vLLM blocks (2309.06180) as substrate; LMCache (2510.09665) as the de facto KV layer, now in the PyTorch Foundation, vLLM production-stack, NVIDIA Dynamo, IBM and CoreWeave, delivering up to 15x throughput with vLLM; Mooncake (2407.00079) as the production disaggregated store (75% more real requests, 100B+ tokens/day). At the bottom tier, Tutti (2605.03375) is current SOTA for SSD-backed KV: vs GDS-enabled SSD-backed LMCache it cuts TTFT 78.3% under strict SLOs, doubles achievable request rate, and reduces serving cost 27% — the cleanest cost number in the slice. On the reuse side, non-prefix reuse is owned by CacheBlend (2405.16444, EuroSys'25, shipped in LMCache: 2.2-3.3x TTFT, 2.8-5x throughput, F1/Rouge-L loss only 0.01-0.03) and its 2026 successors CacheTune (2605.24022, 3.72-4.86x TTFT) and A^3 (2511.17560). Scheduling-side, PEEK (2607.02525) gets up to 3.0x cache-hit improvement on SGLang/vLLM from queue-aware admission alone, and CacheScout (2608.14624) gets +10-18 pp hit rate from learned agent-execution semantics — both with zero accuracy loss.

ECONOMICS SOTA — the actual answer to "what fraction of serving cost is KV memory really?" The literature's answer as of Aug 2026 is: KV memory is not primarily a capacity cost, it is a MOVEMENT cost and a HIT-RATE cost.
- Movement: 2601.19910 defines kappa_crit = (F_pf/B_kv)(BW_PCIe/C_eff) and measures it at ~12-50 (Llama-3.1-405B/H100), 3.1 (Qwen3-235B-A22B/B200), 14.3 (DeepSeek-V3/B200), while real workloads sit at median kappa_ratio 100 (conversation) to 5000 (document QA) — one to three orders of magnitude past the memory-bound threshold. Consequence: 99% of latency in PCIe transfer in the extreme case, 88% in a realistic multi-turn case, PCIe overhead up to 86x prefill compute, and GPUs drawing only 28% of rated TDP (22% on NarrativeQA). Roughly three-quarters of the silicon you are paying for is idle when serving offloaded requests.
- Hit rate: TraceLab (2606.30560) on 4,300 real Claude Code / Codex sessions measures token-weighted prefix hit rates of 95.8% / 95.7%, a ~100:1 cached-to-fresh input ratio (median 126,180 prefix vs 857 append tokens), median output only 252 tokens — and finds prefix tokens are 59.5% of total spend despite ~10x discounted pricing, with human-thinking-time cache misses alone accounting for 12.8% of total priced cost. LMCach

**Collision risk.** Nothing in this slice reproduces your damage law. No offloading / paging / prefix-caching paper I could find does mean-free noise insertion into keys and calibrates a per-model noise-response curve, and none reports an R^2 for predicting perplexity damage across models and configurations. Your asset #3 (per-head shared key mean / normalized-key collapse, causally reproduced by injection) has no analogue at all in this literature. But there are six collisions that are close enough to hurt, ranked by how much damage they do:

1. KVServe (arXiv:2605.13734, 13 May 2026) — HIGHEST RISK, same product, different mechanism. It is 'the first service-aware and adaptive KV communication compression framework', built precisely to answer 'given SLO/quality budgets that drift, which compression configuration should I run?' It solves it with a Bayesian Profiling Engine that searches a modular compression strategy space and distills a 3D Pareto candidate set (claiming 50x reduced offline search), plus an online controller with an analytical latency model and a bandit. If your damage law's pitch is 'pick a safe configuration', KVServe is the incumbent and it is integrated into vLLM with 9.13x/32.8x headline numbers. Your differentiators must be stated up front and are genuinely strong: (a) it profiles, you predict — its Pareto set cannot extrapolate to a bit-width it never profiled, whereas your held-out 5/6/8-bit result within x1.5 is exactly that; (b) it optimizes mean quality under a budget, it has no catastrophe gate; (c) its cost is per-deployment offline search, yours is one 22-point curve per model.

2. arXiv:2607.05399 (Agrawal & Mayer) — benchmarks TurboQuant BY NAME (with KIVI, SnapKV, CaM on Llama-3.1-8B + Mistral-7B), and its headline finding is 'the compression ratio alone is a poor predictor of end-to-end performance.' That is a serving-venue, published, weaker sibling of your atlas headline. Any framing of the form 'the metric everyone reports does not predict real damage' now has a citation ahead of it. Your defence: 176 cells / 26 models / 13 lineages vs 4 methods / 2 models, and — decisively — you supply a predictor where they supply only a negative result. Also note this paper is a live external evaluation of the algorithm your codebase implements; you should read the full text before publishing anything comparative.

3. KV-CoRE (arXiv:2602.05929, 5 Feb 2026) — adjacent slice, same claim shape. 'First comprehensive benchmark examining KV-cache compressibility patterns across LLMs', across models, five English domains and sixteen languages, introducing Normalized Effective Rank and showing it 'correlates with performance loss during compression' / 'correlates strongly with perplexity'. That is: large-scale multi-model atlas + a cheap proxy validated against perplexity damage. Be explicit about the difference or a reviewer will collapse them: NER is a property of the DATA that predicts how compressible a cache is; your curve is a response function of the MODEL that predicts the damage of an arbitrary configuration you never measured, including in the catastrophic regime NER was never tested in (they validate against SVD truncation only).

4. Score space as the correct coordinate is no longer yours to claim. SIFT (arXiv:2606.09441, 8 Jun 2026, published and not withdrawn) is built on measured attention-score invariances — 'the location of high attention scores within a document remain invariant to surrounding documents' — and uses high-attention-score locations as the thing to preserve during RAG prefill reuse. Combined with the withdrawn HeadQ (2605.03562) and with CacheBlend's KV-deviation selection, the 'attention/score space is where fidelity must be measured' idea has independent precedent in a serving venue. Narrow your novelty claim to the calibrated noise->log-damage law and the catastrophic regime; do not claim the coordinate.

5. 'A small set of critical layers carries the damage' is already published in the reuse lit

**Open problems (author-stated).**

- EVALUATION AUDIT — seven measurements nobody reports. From the survey 2607.02574 (Sec 5.5, Table 11): systems fail to report (1) prefix-cache hit rates, (2) KV transfer time/breakdown, (3) metadata memory overhead, (4) eviction/reuse distance, (5) multi-tenant isolation costs, (6) fault-tolerance latency, (7) speculative-decoding KV trajectories. They also state that 'no surveyed system provides durable KV state with an explicit recovery model for node or store failure.' Seven pre-authorized contribution slots, and (1),(2),(4) are measurable on one 4090 against vLLM+LMCache.
- QUALITY LOWER BOUNDS UNDER STRESS — the survey's own words are your abstract. 2607.08057 (Findings of ACL 2026), challenge C3: 'Structural methods can harm robustness in ways standard metrics miss, as policies may evict or compress low-salience but crucial context, causing severe errors under workload shifts despite stable mean accuracy.' They call for work that will 'expose worst-case failures and quality lower bounds under stress, making trustworthiness behavior-attributed and SLO-aware.' A peer-reviewed survey is asking, in print, for exactly a calibrated catastrophe gate.
- CROSS-BEHAVIOR CO-OPTIMIZATION. 2607.08057 challenge C5: 'most sKis optimize behaviors in isolation, despite their interactions under bandwidth and latency constraints' — i.e. eviction, offload and prefetch decisions are made independently. And C1: 'Under long contexts and bursts, KV generation, migration, and compression may trigger SLO violations, which often arise from cross-behavior interference rather than a single operation.'
- BENCHMARKING INCOMPARABILITY. 2607.08057 challenge C6 calls for 'unified sKis benchmarks', citing 'inconsistent metric definitions and measures across tools, preventing reliable apples-to-apples comparisons.' Independently, 2607.05399 concludes 'the compression ratio alone is a poor predictor of end-to-end performance' and 2604.08426 ends with: 'These findings highlight the need for a comprehensive and rigorous evaluation of long-context compression techniques.'
- OFFLOADING'S ACCURACY FAILURES ARE DIAGNOSED, NOT PREDICTED. 2604.08426 identifies two root causes — 'low-rank key compression [becomes] inaccurate' on context-intensive tasks and 'group-based KV selection is inaccurate on context-intensive tasks, and this is not easily mitigated with outliers' — with ShadowKV falling 46.75% -> 10.46% on Text2JSON (Llama-3.1-8B). They disclaim scope: 'The main focus of our work is to better understand the effectiveness of KV offloading, not design the highest performance algorithm', and 'Our analysis is also limited to models trained with full attention: models with alternative attention types such as MLA or Gated DeltaNet present interesting directions for future research.' Nobody can predict, before running, whether a given offloading config will fall off this cliff.
- OFFLOADING BOTTLENECK MODEL IS COARSE AND CALLS FOR HARDWARE IT DOES NOT HAVE. 2601.19910 admits: 'Our framework approximates F_pf using 2N FLOPs per token, omitting the context scaling term' (~10% error at very long contexts). Its proposed fixes are largely not available to a solo researcher — 'NVLink Chip-to-Chip (C2C) interconnect with 900 GB/s unidirectional bandwidth' (5.3x kappa_crit), unified memory (9x further), MLA (2.7-4.7x KV reduction) — but its scheduling recommendation is: 'Schedulers should maximize compute utilization within VRAM constraints' rather than pure token accounting. Nobody has built that scheduler with a quality term in it.
- OFFLOAD-VS-PREFILL CROSSOVER IS UNMODELLED. LMCache (2510.09665) states the open decision plainly: 'under low bandwidth, loading should be enabled only when the context length surpasses the crossover point where loading becomes faster than prefilling.' They also expose an unsolved allocation tradeoff: 'a smaller duplication window reduces the duplication ratio but increases the likelihood of allocation stalls.' And a workload-level open problem: context truncation, 'a widely applied technique in industry, can greatly reduce prefix cache hit ratio by half' (~85% -> ~45%).
- COMPRESSION MAY BE NET-NEGATIVE ON THE SERVING PATH. ShadowServe (2509.16857) opens with: 'Compression mitigates the bandwidth issue, but can degrade overall performance when decompression interferes with model computation' (they measure both tasks slowing >=30% when co-located on one GPU). 2503.24000 adds that current KV-compression implementations 'lack production-level optimization' against FlashAttention/PagedAttention and that 'compressing KV cache may lead to longer outputs, resulting in increased end-to-end latency.' Nobody has published a decompression-cost-aware bit allocation.
- WORKLOAD CHARACTERIZATION IS THIN AND SELF-ADMITTEDLY NARROW. TraceLab (2606.30560) limitations: the dataset 'derives from their own development workflows rather than broader organizational patterns', the analysis 'relies on external logs without internal provider telemetry', and the scope 'remains narrowly on coding agents rather than broader computer-use applications'. They propose extending the methodology to other agent domains and larger deployments. There is no public, model-diverse workload+quality trace.
- PER-SAMPLE RATHER THAN AGGREGATE ACCOUNTING. 2503.24000 reports that examining 'accuracy performance of individual samples rather than the overall performance' reveals intrinsic limitations invisible in means — a direct invitation for a per-configuration worst-case predictor rather than a mean-quality report.
- REUSE HETEROGENEITY IS MEASURED BUT NOT EXPLOITED FOR PRECISION. SAECache (2605.18825) measures 'up to 756x variation in reuse rates' across token types (system prompt / user query / tool output / model response / CoT), and uses it only for eviction ordering — not for how many bits each block should get.

**Key works.**

- `arXiv:2601.19910` (16 Dec 2025 (submitted to MLSys 2026); Meng (UPenn/Intel), Lee, Wang) **Understanding Bottlenecks for Efficiently Serving LLM Inference With KV Offloading** — Analytical + empirical characterization of KV offloading over PCIe. Derives kappa_crit = (F_pf / B_kv) x (BW_PCIe / C_eff), the cached-to-prefill token ratio above which execution goes memory-bound. Measured kappa_crit: ~50 for Llama-3.1-405B on H100/PCIe5 at peak BW, ~12 at the measured 15 GB/s sus *Relevance:* This is the single best published answer to your slice question. It says KV memory's cost is NOT capacity, it is movement: an offloaded serving GPU is idle 72% of its power budget waiting on PCIe. That reframes what a 5.54x compressor buys — bytes on the wire and cache residency, not 'longer context
- `arXiv:2510.09665` (8 Oct 2025 (v1), 5 Dec 2025 (v2); Liu, Cheng, Yao, ... Jiang (UChicago)) **LMCache: An Efficient KV Cache Layer for Enterprise-Scale LLM Inference** — The de facto open-source KV cache layer (PyTorch Foundation Oct 2025; in vLLM production-stack, NVIDIA Dynamo, IBM stack, CoreWeave). Extracts KV out of GPU memory, shares across engines and queries; supports prefix reuse offloading and PD-disaggregation transfer. Batched data movement, compute/IO p *Relevance:* This is the integration surface any non-quantization KV work now has to land on, and the source of the best public hit-rate statistics. The 85%->45% truncation finding is a workload-level result you can build on: it means retained-cache capacity (what compression buys) directly moves hit rate.
- `arXiv:2407.00079` (24 Jun 2024 (v1), 3 Sep 2025 (v4); Qin et al., Moonshot AI; USENIX FAST'25 + ACM TOS) **Mooncake: A KVCache-centric Disaggregated Architecture for LLM Serving** — Production platform behind Kimi. Separates prefill and decode clusters; pools the GPU cluster's underused CPU, DRAM, SSD, NIC into a disaggregated KVCache store with a KVCache-centric scheduler and prediction-based early rejection under overload. Up to 525% simulated throughput gain; 75% more reques *Relevance:* Establishes the industrial premise: KV cache is treated as a first-class distributed storage object, not a per-request tensor. Any compression claim gets judged against 'we already got the win by pooling idle DRAM/SSD for free'.
- `arXiv:2309.06180` (12 Sep 2023; Kwon, Li, Zhuang, Sheng, Zheng, Yu, Gonzalez, Zhang, Stoica (vLLM)) **Efficient Memory Management for Large Language Model Serving with PagedAttention** — OS-style paging of the KV cache into fixed blocks; near-zero KV memory waste, block-level sharing within and across requests (the substrate that made prefix caching possible). 2-4x throughput over FasterTransformer/Orca, larger gains at longer sequences. *Relevance:* Baseline of the whole slice, and the reason 'save memory by compressing' competes with 'save memory by not fragmenting it'. Your bit-packed cache must be paged-block-compatible to be adoptable.
- `arXiv:2405.04437` (7 May 2024 (v1), 29 Jan 2025 (v3); Prabhu, Nayak, Mohan, Ramjee, Panwar (MSR India)) **vAttention: Dynamic Memory Management for Serving LLMs without PagedAttention** — Keeps KV contiguous in virtual memory while decoupling physical allocation via CUDA VMM APIs, avoiding PagedAttention's non-contiguous layout and the attention-kernel rewrites it forces. Up to 1.23x throughput over PagedAttention kernels of FlashAttention/FlashInfer. *Relevance:* Direct evidence for a cost you will pay: any custom KV representation (packed 3-bit + sign residuals) forces a custom attention kernel. vAttention exists precisely because that tax is real and measurable.
- `arXiv:2605.03375` (5 May 2026; Qiu, Hu, Wang, Zhu, Yan, Chen, Xu, Chen, Zhang) **Tutti: Making SSD-Backed KV Cache Practical for Long-Context LLM Serving** — GPU-centric KV object store on NVMe. Removes the CPU from the data and I/O control path (GPU io_uring, GPU-native object abstraction, slack-aware I/O scheduling); CPU only asynchronously loads I/O kernels once per layer. vs SOTA GDS-enabled SSD-backed LMCache: 78.3% lower TTFT under strict SLOs, 2x  *Relevance:* Current SOTA for the bottom tier. The '27% serving cost reduction' is one of the few explicit cost deltas in the literature — it is your comparator number if you claim compression saves serving cost. Also: if SSD-backed KV is near-free, the marginal value of 5x compression is capacity for MORE prefi
- `arXiv:2511.11907` (14 Nov 2025 (v1), 11 Dec 2025 (v2); Zhang, Xia, Wang (Leeds)) **KVSwap: Disk-aware KV Cache Offloading for Long-Context On-device Inference** — Offloading for unified-memory mobile/embedded devices where CPU and GPU/NPU share memory and disk bandwidth is scarce: full cache on disk, compact in-memory metadata to predict preloads, computation/IO overlap, read patterns matched to device characteristics. Higher throughput under tight memory bud *Relevance:* Closest analogue to your single-4090 constraint (one memory pool, no cluster). Shows the accepted framing for resource-constrained KV work — and that it is already occupied on the systems side.
- `arXiv:2603.27138` (28 Mar 2026; Zhang, Zhou, Tang, Lu, Li, Yang, Xu, Wan) **ScoutAttention: Efficient KV Cache Offloading via Layer-Ahead CPU Pre-computation for LLM Inference** — GPU-CPU collaborative block-wise sparse attention with a layer-ahead pre-computation algorithm (CPU starts attention one layer early) plus asynchronous periodic recall. Accuracy within 2.4% of baseline, 2.1x speedup over existing offloading methods. *Relevance:* Representative of the 2026 offloading frontier: sparsity + scheduling, not compression. Note it reports accuracy loss as a single scalar ('within 2.4%') on chosen tasks — exactly the kind of under-specified quality claim your damage law could replace.
- `arXiv:2604.08426` (9 Apr 2026; Bocharnikov, Ermakov, Kuznedelev, Zhdanovskiy, Yershov (HSE / Yandex)) **KV Cache Offloading for Context-Intensive Tasks** — Evaluation paper. Releases Text2JSON, a deliberately context-intensive benchmark, and evaluates five offloading systems (ShadowKV, ArkVale, LRQK, InfiniGen, plus their minimal YAKV). Finds severe degradation on Llama-3 and Qwen-3 — e.g. ShadowKV drops 46.75% -> 10.46% on Text2JSON with Llama-3.1-8B. *Relevance:* The most important paper in this slice for you. It independently establishes that standard offloading benchmarks hide catastrophic failures, and that the failure is in KEY approximation fidelity — the same mechanism family your damage law measures. It takes the novelty out of the *motivation* for a 
- `arXiv:2405.16444` (26 May 2024 (v1), 3 Apr 2025 (v3); Yao, Li, Liu, Ray, Cheng, Zhang, Du, Lu, Jiang; EuroSys'25, ACM TOCS 2025) **CacheBlend: Fast Large Language Model Serving for RAG with Cached Knowledge Fusion** — Reuses precomputed KV for non-prefix chunks and selectively recomputes a small subset of tokens (chosen by KV deviation) to restore cross-attention. Recompute is pipelined with cache retrieval, so caches can live on slow/large devices. 2.2-3.3x TTFT reduction, 2.8-5x throughput, F1/Rouge-L loss 0.01 *Relevance:* The canonical 'reuse a slightly-wrong KV cache and repair it' method. Its selection criterion is a KV-space deviation metric — the direct competitor coordinate to your score-space noise. If your score-space coordinate is genuinely better, CacheBlend's HKVD selection is the obvious head-to-head exper
- `arXiv:2605.24022` (20 May 2026; F. Li, Liu, Liu, Cui, Nie, Wang, Wu) **CacheTune: Adaptive KV Cache Reuse for Fast Long-Context LLM Serving** — Non-prefix reuse system. Offline frequency-domain analysis identifies the KV pairs most critical to cross-attention recovery; online it recomputes only those, plus sparse KV transfer, multi-stream overlap, deferred positional-encoding recovery, and hardware-aware adaptive recompute-ratio tuning acro *Relevance:* Two collisions in one: (1) an offline-computed criticality profile driving an online reuse decision — structurally the same product as your damage law; (2) the 'critical layers' result means 'a small set of layers carries the damage' is already published in the reuse literature, so your worst-layer 
- `arXiv:2606.09441` (8 Jun 2026; Sanovar, Bharadwaj, Taneja, Qureshi (Georgia Tech / MSR)) **SIFT: Selective-Index For Fast Compute of RAG Prefill by Exploiting Attention Invariance** — Exploits two measured invariances — local-attention invariance ('the location of high attention scores within a document remain invariant to surrounding documents') and cross-attention consistency ('keys with high intra-document attention also attract cross-attention from subsequent documents') — to *Relevance:* Score-space-as-the-correct-coordinate, published, verified, not withdrawn. Combined with the withdrawn HeadQ this means your 'score space is the right coordinate' framing has independent precedent in a serving venue. Your defensible claim narrows to the calibrated noise->damage law, not the coordina

---

### Evaluation, metrics and benchmarks — slice: alignment, safety and behavioural degradation under quantization/compression; perplexity-invisible damage.

This slice went from near-empty in 2024 to crowd

**State of the art.** Three distinct fronts, each with a defining number.

1. DETECTING perplexity-invisible safety damage under KV-cache quantization — SOTA is PCR (arXiv:2606.09864). A 20-prompt, ~35-GPU-minute, training-free pre-deployment diagnostic that sorts a model into one of three mechanistic failure modes and predicts the correct mitigation direction on 9/9 primary models and 1 held-out family, recovering up to 97% of lost refusals (97.2% under KIVI) at 0-7% memory overhead. The problem it defines: Mistral-7B loses 15.2% of refusals at 1.03x perplexity; Qwen-2.5-7B hits 90.3% ConditionalFlip at 4-bit; production vLLM fp8_e5m2 gives 30.3% ConditionalFlip on Qwen. Critically, PCR predicts *direction, not magnitude* — that is the stated gap.

2. QUANTITATIVELY PREDICTING behavioural damage from a fitted law — SOTA is multiplicative margin shrinkage (arXiv:2608.06564): median 1.7 percentage-point error on held-out flip rates and 0.004 calibration error across 161,744 predictions, over 16 models / 8 families / 8-to-2 bits, with no labelled training data. Margin factors: 0.86 at 4 bits, 0.33 at 3 bits, 0.00 at 2 bits. This is the number any new "damage law" gets compared against, and it already carries a KV-cache arm (Appendix A.9) and a safety instrument.

3. RUNTIME GATING on KV-cache distortion — SOTA is WitCert (arXiv:2607.28699): a sound per-(layer, head, step) upper bound on the total variation between exact and compressed attention, at 32 bytes per token per head, wired into SGLang, delivering 1.88x more KV tokens at equal memory with the paired difference from uncompressed bounded to [+0.0, +0.8] on RULER.

Consensus of the slice, now stated in at least five independent 2026 preprints: perplexity, KLD, accuracy and closed-form safety benchmarks are all decoupled from real behavioural damage under compression, the decoupling is model-specific with sharp phase transitions, and there is no universal safe bit-width. Two papers go further and report perplexity *improving* while internal structure degrades (arXiv:2606.03002, INT7 improves PPL 5.65% while 18.7% of active SAE features degrade).

**Collision risk.** HARD COLLISIONS — treat these as novelty-threatening, in descending severity.

1. arXiv:2606.09864 (Alignment Collapse, v1 2026-06-01, v2 2026-08-08) collides on FOUR separate assets at once, and it is dated before the 2026-08-18 sprint.
   (a) THE QWEN2.5 CATASTROPHE IS ALREADY PUBLISHED, WITH PERPLEXITY NUMBERS. Their Appendix Table 13 (WikiText-103, KV-cache quantization) reads: Qwen-2.5-7B 6.52 (16-bit) -> 6.90 (8-bit) -> 1803.20 (6-bit) -> 44985.48 (4-bit) -> 69766.64 (3-bit), while all ten other models (DeepSeek-7B, Gemma-2-9B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B, Mixtral-8x7B, Mistral-Small-24B, Phi-3.5-mini, Qwen-2.5-72B, Yi-1.5-9B, Yi-1.5-34B) sit near baseline at 6-bit. A family-specific catastrophic Qwen2.5 KV failure, measured end-to-end in perplexity, against a 10-model control group, is prior art as of June 2026. The claim "we discovered the Qwen2.5 KV catastrophe" is dead. What survives: the MECHANISM (shared per-head key mean; k_proj bias at boundary layers + massive activations in middle layers), the LINEAGE arc (Qwen1.5 immune -> Qwen2 catastrophic -> Qwen3 intermediate -> Qwen3.5 immune, five generations), and the CAUSAL INJECTION (reproducing it on demand in an immune model). They offer no explanation of the PPL explosion at all — it is an unremarked table entry inside a safety paper, and their own mechanism (safety-subspace geometry) does not explain it. Note also a genuine disagreement worth exploiting: their Qwen-2.5-72B does NOT blow up at 6-bit (3.73), so the pathology is not uniform across the Qwen2.5 family in their data.
   (b) LAYER-0 PROTECTION. "Qwen-2.5-7B (concentrated). Protecting L0-1 at FP16 achieves 97.8% recovery on AdvBench." This is the third independent arrival at first-layer protection for Qwen2.5, after llama.cpp#21385 and our own worst-layer profile (L0 = 0.5488, L27 = 0.80). The finding is now over-determined; publish it as convergence, never as discovery.
   (c) THE PAIRED ATLAS. 11 models x 7 bit-widths with perplexity AND behavioural damage recorded per cell is structurally the same artifact as the 176-cell atlas, and it is on the stronger axis (behaviour, not perplexity).
   (d) THE PRE-DEPLOYMENT CHECKER. PCR is a 20-prompt, ~35-GPU-minute, training-free, before-you-deploy diagnostic for KV quantization. That is kvcheck.py's product niche, already occupied, already validated on 9+1 models, already shown to generalize to KIVI and vLLM FP8.
   NOT collided by them: no noise injection, no quantizer-free calibration, no continuous magnitude prediction, no score-space/attention-logit coordinate.

2. arXiv:2608.06564 (Which Decisions Low-Bit Quantization Breaks / "Quantization Damage Is Multiplicative, Not Additive", 2026-08-06, v3 2026-08-13) collides on the DAMAGE LAW's rhetorical position. It is a fitted, calibration-free, cross-model law for quantization damage with strong held-out validation (median 1.7 pp error on held-out flip rates; 0.004 calibration error over 161,744 predictions; 16 models / 8 families), it explicitly covers a KV-cache axis, and it uses safety instruments. It also lands on the budget-inversion result: Appendix A.9 concludes "the usual allocation is inverted" — KV cache needs more bits than usually given. Any reviewer who knows this paper will ask, in one sentence, what the damage law adds. Defensible answers: their observable is a decision margin over an enumerated option set (needs a task), ours is a scalar read off one forward pass (task-free); their response is a flip rate on forced single-token choices, ours is end-to-end perplexity over a x1348 dynamic range; they do not calibrate by noise injection; their KV work is one appendix. Undefensible: claiming novelty for "quantization damage obeys a fitted per-model law with held-out validation".

3. arXiv:2606.19558 (Displacement Is Not Direction, 2026-06-17) collides on asset #2's headline. "Cheap fidelity proxy is uncorrelated with real damage" is already published as a regime result with better statistics: rho -0.7

**Open problems (author-stated).**

- arXiv:2606.09864 (Alignment Collapse), Appendix H, verbatim: 'PCR predicts direction, not always magnitude. PCR correctly identifies the dominant failure mechanism for all tested models, but does not fully account for inter-layer interactions.' — There is NO published method that predicts the MAGNITUDE of KV-quantization safety damage. That is the single largest hole in this slice and the exact shape of the damage law.
- arXiv:2606.09864, Section 7, verbatim: 'any inference-time approximation perturbing critical-layer representations should exhibit PCR-aligned failure; extending the protocol to pruning, eviction, low-rank compression, and rotation-based quantizers is future work.' They explicitly have not tested SmoothQuant or QuaRot-style rotations: 'Other outlier-aware methods not yet tested include SmoothQuant... and QuaRot-style rotation methods.' — Our WHT-rotation + Lloyd-Max + mean-removal cache is exactly an untested rotation-based quantizer, and mean-removal is the outlier-redistribution operation they hypothesize will shift models between PCR regimes.
- arXiv:2606.09864, Limitations, verbatim: 'For models with very low per-layer flip rates (Mixtral-8x7B, Yi-1.5-34B), the calibration set should be increased from 20 to 50 prompts to obtain sufficient signal.' — Their calibration instrument is prompt-hungry and signal-starved on robust models. A quantizer-free noise-injection curve does not have this failure mode, because it manufactures its own signal at arbitrary magnitude.
- arXiv:2606.19558 (Displacement Is Not Direction), verbatim: 'The silent-zone thresholds are descriptive and cohort-specific: they depend on the reference model, quantization methods, benchmark suite, and KLD setup... Our analysis is limited to two model families and a LiveCodeBench-focused routing study... In practice, fidelity metrics should be treated as diagnostics rather than model-ranking criteria.' — They state the thresholds are cohort-specific and offer no way to derive a model's own threshold. That is precisely what a per-model calibrated curve supplies.
- arXiv:2608.06564 (Which Decisions Low-Bit Quantization Breaks), Limitations, verbatim: 'Post-training quantization; decisions read at the first token, single-step; five models on the activation axis, sixteen on the weight axis... The free-generation check that anchors the margin to behaviour was run on the weight axis only.' Plus the stated unknowns: 'Why large models preserve larger margin fractions remains unexplained' and 'The causal mechanisms connecting internal disturbances to margin collapse need investigation.' — The KV axis is one appendix, single-step first-token decisions only, and the mechanism is openly unexplained. Our mechanism (shared per-head key mean collapsing normalized keys) is a candidate causal account of exactly the internal disturbance they cannot name.
- arXiv:2606.10154 (Quality Is Not a Safety Proxy), Limitations, verbatim: 'The matrix spans 6 models in 4 families, with the largest model in the 7B class.' and 'The safety battery covers AdvBench refusal, TruthfulQA, BBQ, and a jailbreak-amplification slice under single-turn, fixed-decoding conditions. It does not cover privacy leakage, multi-turn strategic attacks, prompt extraction, or non-English prompts.' Future work, verbatim: 'mechanism-focused work could test refusal-direction magnitude, token-flipping interventions, or attribution graphs on the specific hidden-danger rows.' — They name mechanism work on refusal-direction magnitude as the next step and have not done it. Small models, one 7B ceiling — a 24 GB box is not a disadvantage here.
- arXiv:2607.21063 (QuantiBias), Limitations, verbatim: 'Activation precision is held constant here and is a second treatment dimension this stage does not vary; it requires hardware unavailable on the current cluster.' and 'Power analysis against the observed effect size (Cohen's h~0.15) indicates several hundred to above one thousand paired prompts per cell are required to resolve the per-family slope.' and 'The screening probe is sensitive to the fast path by design (reasoning off); families whose degradation lives elsewhere would be missed.' — Behavioural safety measurement is statistically expensive (hundreds to >1000 prompts per cell). This is the strongest practical argument for a cheap noise-curve predictor: the ground truth it would replace costs 10^3 prompts per cell.
- arXiv:2606.03002 (Perplexity Can Miss SAE Feature Damage), Future Work, verbatim: 'We release our pipeline and per-feature results, and view causal validation as the next step: testing whether these feature changes alter steering, auditing, or other safety-relevant interventions in deployed quantized models.' Limitations, verbatim: 'Our study is limited to two model families, publicly available residual-stream SAEs, and simulated round-to-nearest weight quantization... the quantitative survival rates should not be interpreted as universal.' — The causal link from measured internal damage to behavioural consequence is explicitly unclaimed. Our causal-injection technique (reproducing the pathology on demand in an immune model) is the method that closes exactly this kind of gap.
- arXiv:2607.28699 (WitCert), Section 5 Limitations, verbatim: 'The certificate is a per-layer, per-head local guarantee... Tier-B probabilistic guarantee is stated for non-adaptive queries only' and 'certificate aggregation under multi-GPU / tensor parallelism is unverified; model coverage is three 6-7B-class GQA families'. Section 6.4.6 lists six open problems, including: better constant-size witnesses for sub-8-bit schemes; certified compression for token-eviction methods; martingale-style adaptive analysis for free decoding; conformally calibrated gate thresholds on live traffic. — Their bound is LOCAL per-head and they cannot aggregate it to an end-to-end damage statement. That is the composition problem a worst-layer empirical law sidesteps, and 'sub-8-bit witnesses' is open — our whole regime is 1-4 bit.
- arXiv:2606.17872 (AnchorKV), verbatim: 'Whether the test AUROC of 0.996 already saturates or could be further improved by a low-rank projection or a small mixture of anchors is an open question.' and 'The anchor is constructed and validated on the AdvBench-paraphrase distribution... not necessarily on out-of-distribution benign prompts.' Single target model (Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct). — A single linear safety direction in KEY SPACE, validated on one model. Whether a large shared per-head key mean corrupts that direction is an untested, directly reachable question on our hardware.
- arXiv:2407.04965 (Beyond Perplexity), Limitations, verbatim: '...our evaluations focus on 7B and 13B-sized models and their compressed models. The bias, toxicity, and performance evaluations with compressed larger models, such as 30B and 70B Llama and Tulu models remain to be studied.' Conclusions, verbatim: 'do not just measure perplexity or safety, always use both' and 'aggregated metrics for safety can hide the nuanced movement across different protected groups and dialects, and it is imperative to conduct fine-grained evaluation.' — The standing community recommendation is that any compression evaluation must report BOTH perplexity and safety. A perplexity-only atlas is non-compliant with the field's own stated norm.
- arXiv:2607.09999 (Silent Failures in Quantized LLM Reasoning): standard evaluation metrics cannot reliably detect Hollow Convergence, 'best F1 = 0.53' — a stated open problem: no metric yet detects reasoning-process degradation under quantization.
- arXiv:2608.11212 (Route Flip): 'no predictor of a flip's loss sign above chance' among observable router statistics — detecting that damage occurred is easy (AUC 0.772); knowing whether it hurts is unsolved. The same sign problem is unaddressed for KV-cache perturbations.
- arXiv:2607.28196 (Fidelity Is Not Safety), central claim, verbatim: 'The governing axis is the coherence of the compression error times its rate; the magnitude of the damage does not predict it. The data-free fidelity probe is a fidelity oracle by construction, so it cannot see this axis.' — Stated as an open blind spot of ALL magnitude-based screens. This is simultaneously the biggest threat to a scalar-noise damage law and the most valuable thing to test: does a coherence x rate term explain the 1-bit centered residual that four noise geometries all failed to explain?

**Key works.**

---

### Reproducibility, replication failure, benchmark-gaming critique, and reproduction infrastructure in the LLM inference-efficiency literature — specifically as it bears on KV-cache compression and quant

**State of the art.** There is no single SOTA "method" here — the territory's state of the art is a set of evaluation *protocols*, and it has hardened sharply in 2026.

For third-party audits of KV-cache compression, the current bar is set by arXiv:2607.11942 (Matched-Budget Audit, July 2026): every arm at the same retained-KV budget, every contrast paired per instance, 50,000-resample bootstrap CIs, trivial baselines as the anchor rather than published baselines, explicit measurement and disclosure of confounds, public withdrawal of any claim a confound swallows, and release of all per-instance records (185,700 in their case). Its defining numbers are the two hazards it quantified: a -0.29 (pooled -0.221) RULER accuracy shift from swapping sdpa for eager attention on an *uncompressed* model — larger than most method-vs-baseline gaps in the literature — and a tokenizer-dependent overflow that silently zeroed 7 of 13 RULER subtasks at nominal "8192" on gemma-2. KVDiagnosis (arXiv:2608.09412, Aug 2026) sets the parallel bar for diagnostic corpora: 59,800 supported runs, 12,520 paired FullCache-correct/compressed-wrong rows, stratified AUROC 0.684-0.871 for ten diagnostics, sham-controlled causal interventions, and public exclusion of one method (PyramidKV) whose adapter failed an implementation audit.

For cross-stack reproducibility of any LLM number, the defining figure is 16.6 percentage points — the benchmark swing attributable to inference backend alone, from a survey of 200 engines and 35,000 publications (arXiv:2605.19537). For quantization evaluation specifically, the defining figure is 500,000+ evaluations (arXiv:2411.02355 / Neural Magic), whose conclusion is that most reported quantization damage is an artifact of oversensitive evaluation, chat-template formatting, and untuned quantizer hyperparameters.

For infrastructure, the state of the art is NVIDIA kvpress (30+ methods behind one hook API, public HF leaderboard) as the de facto reference implementation that third-party audits now pin by version, plus the MBE Matched-Budget Evaluation protocol (fixed retained-KV-memory budget ladder 50/25/12.5/6.25%, mandatory task grid, mandatory system metrics, PR-submitted "KV Compression Cards" rendered to a public leaderboard). For venues, MLRC became an official NeurIPS track in 2026 and explicitly values documented failures to reproduce as first-class contributions.

The territory's own headline finding, stated independently by at least four 2026 works, is that compression 

**Collision risk.** Ranked most to least dangerous.

1. arXiv:2608.08684 (RippleKV, 2026-08-09 — NINE DAYS AGO). Injects norm-adaptive perturbations into each layer's KV cache, measures induced KL at the output over a small calibration set, and derives a per-model sensitivity profile that "need not vary monotonically with depth." This is noise-injection-as-per-model-calibration for KV, published. You can no longer claim that primitive. What survives: keys rather than values; score-space (worst-layer attention-logit correlation) as the coordinate rather than output KL; a *fitted* log-damage law with R^2 0.971 rather than a normalized score used for budget allocation; extrapolation to held-out bit-widths; a calibrated catastrophe gate. Reframe the contribution as "a transferable damage law in score-space coordinates," never as "we inject noise to calibrate."

2. arXiv:2606.09864 (Alignment Collapse Under KV Cache Quantization, June 2026, rev Aug 2026). Structurally your entire package: 11-model multi-lineage measured atlas (3.8B-72B, 5 benchmarks, 1,894 prompts) + a geometric mechanism (safety features in a low-dimensional subspace 10^2-10^3x more noise-vulnerable) + a cheap diagnostic (PCR, ~35 GPU-minutes, 20 calibration prompts) that predicts the correct mitigation on all nine primary models AND one held-out model from an independent family, generalizing to a production quantizer (KIVI, up to 97.2% recovery). It even reports "sharp model-specific phase transitions invisible to standard metrics." The only structural differences are the damage axis (refusal rate vs perplexity) and that PCR is a three-way classifier rather than a continuous law with an R^2. Worse: their headline (15.2% refusal loss at 1.03x perplexity) is a direct argument that a perplexity-calibrated law is blind to a damage mode that matters. You must pre-empt this in your own limitations section or a reviewer will use it against you.

3. arXiv:2604.19528 (Revisiting RaBitQ and TurboQuant, April 2026). Not a collision with your assets — a collision with your *foundation*. It reports that TurboQuant loses to RaBitQ on most inner-product, ANN, and KV-quantization settings, and that several published TurboQuant runtime/recall numbers "could not be reproduced from the released implementation under the stated configuration." Everything you have built is branded TurboQuantDC. Anyone reviewing your work will find this within one search. Two required responses: never cite TurboQuant's published numbers as ground truth, and consider whether "TurboQuant" should remain in the name of the artifact you publish.

4. arXiv:2604.18085 (Predicting LLM Compression Degradation from Spectral Statistics, April 2026, solo author). Same contribution *shape* as your damage law: a cheap statistic predicts compression damage before you spend the eval compute, packaged as an explicit "predict-then-compress workflow." Claimed four months ahead of you. Their scope (low-rank weight compression, two model families, LOO Pearson 0.890/0.839) is narrower and weaker than yours (KV quantization, 13 lineages, 96 configs, R^2 0.971, held-out bit-widths, catastrophe gate), but the *novelty of the idea* "predict degradation without running the eval" is now partly spent. Position against it explicitly and by number, in the abstract.

5. arXiv:2608.09412 (KVDiagnosis, Aug 2026) + arXiv:2607.05399 (Benchmarking KV-Cache Optimizations, May 2026). Together these occupy the "large measured grid of proxy diagnostics vs real end-to-end outcomes for KV compression" ground that your 176-cell atlas claims. KVDiagnosis publishes 59,800 runs / 12,520 paired failure rows with ten diagnostics at AUROC 0.684-0.871 and sham-controlled interventions; 2607.05399 already benchmarks TurboQuant head-to-head against KIVI/SnapKV/CaM and already published the conclusion that compression ratio is a poor end-to-end predictor. Your atlas is not novel as an atlas. It is novel only as the *calibration set for a predictive law*. Sell it that way.

6

**Open problems (author-stated).**

- Matched-Budget Audit (arXiv:2607.11942), on their central mechanism: 'We advance this as a mechanistic hypothesis - ordinal evidence over six methods, not a fitted law.' They explicitly leave the fitted quantitative law undone: 'the manipulations that would test it' are named but not run. This is the single clearest stated opening for a fitted, R^2-bearing damage law in this territory.
- Matched-Budget Audit (arXiv:2607.11942), on scope: 'We withdraw the ranking claim for H2O: its deficit is observationally inseparable from a backend term larger than itself.' Their backend control was 'measured on one model and arm' and excluded the three longest subtasks because eager's O(L^2) fp32 softmax exhausts 48 GB at L~11k. A systematic, multi-model backend-confound characterization remains unwritten - and is single-GPU feasible.
- Matched-Budget Audit (arXiv:2607.11942), coverage gap: 'KVzip's multi-pass compression procedure does not fit a single-pass matched-budget harness, and Compactor is not implemented in the audited kvpress release (both are natural extensions).' They also note their audit contains no quantization arms at all - it is eviction/pruning only. Quantizers under a matched-budget audit protocol is an open slot.
- Benchmarking KV-Cache Optimizations (arXiv:2607.05399): 'The results show that the compression ratio alone is a poor predictor of end-to-end performance... These findings motivate workload-aware selection of KV-cache mechanisms rather than one-size-fits-all compression.' They stop at 'select per workload' - they do not offer any predictor. A cheap predictor of end-to-end damage is the stated missing piece.
- KVDiagnosis (arXiv:2608.09412): 'Under fixed diagnostic rules, 63.2% have low or partial measured/projected coverage. Only 19 rows (0.2%) combine high measured/projected coverage with strong likelihood drift.' And from the repo: for structural methods (ThinK, QuantizedCache) 'their positions remain addressable, but representation fidelity is unknown, so numeric ERR/ECov is not reported.' Representation-fidelity diagnostics for quantization-family methods are explicitly unmeasured - which is exactly the family your score-space coordinate covers.
- KVDiagnosis repo caveat: 'The 75/50/25 labels are configured KV ratio settings. They are not guaranteed to represent byte-equivalent memory footprints across token selection, channel compression, and quantization methods.' Byte-equivalent budget accounting across mechanism families is an admitted, unsolved reporting problem.
- The Pitfalls of KV Cache Compression (arXiv:2510.00231): 'we provide a more skeptical view... the true cost of KV cache compression is poorly understood. In fact, the impacts of compression can be very unpredictable... making it difficult to predict which instructions will be followed and which will be discarded.' Predictability is named as the open problem, in an ACL 2026 long paper.
- Alignment Collapse (arXiv:2606.09864): 'no universal safe bit width exists, with sharp model-specific phase transitions invisible to standard metrics,' and 'Mistral-7B loses 15.2% of its refusals at only 1.03x perplexity.' Stated implication: perplexity-based damage prediction provably does not cover safety damage. Extending any perplexity-calibrated law to the alignment axis is open - and is a stated hole in perplexity-based methods generally.
- Two Failure Modes of LLM Quantization (arXiv:2604.19884): 'targeted, training-free repair can mitigate Signal Degradation, but remains ineffective for Computation Collapse... addressing Computation Collapse requires structural reconstruction rather than mere compensation.' Whether a single continuous damage curve can span both regimes is unaddressed by them and is the obvious challenge to any x0.99-to-x1348 single-law claim.
- Physics of KV Cache Compression (arXiv:2603.01426): 'we argue these evaluations miss a structural issue,' and their finding that 'probe scores can drop early while generation stays stable until the cliff' - i.e. cheap internal probes systematically mis-time the failure. Any proxy-based predictor must demonstrate it does not fire early in the pre-cliff redundancy regime; they do not solve this, they document it.
- Predicting LLM Compression Degradation from Spectral Statistics (arXiv:2604.18085): confined to matrix-level low-rank weight compression on Qwen3 and Gemma3 only, with LOO Pearson 0.890/0.839. The KV-cache setting, quantization (as opposed to low-rank), and cross-lineage generalization are all outside their demonstrated scope - the author states the workflow ambition ('predict-then-compress') far more broadly than the evidence.
- The Silent Hyperparameter (arXiv:2605.19537): across 35,000 analysed publications, 'inference stack specifications are rarely documented,' and backend choice alone shifts scores up to 16.6 pp. They recommend standardized reporting but provide no adopted standard, no normalization procedure, and no way to reconcile already-published numbers across stacks.
- ReproRepo (arXiv:2606.18237): the best agent 'surfaces at least one semantically related human-reported blocker for ~90% of papers' but agents remain 'insufficient in exact localization.' Automated reproducibility auditing cannot yet pinpoint the failing component.
- MLRC 2026 organizers (NeurIPS blog, 2026-05-04): 'reproducibility is not a binary outcome: a paper is never simply reproducible or not... A careful, well-documented failure to reproduce a result... is a genuine contribution to the literature.' They are explicitly soliciting graded, mechanism-level reproduction studies - the format is defined but the KV/quantization instances of it are largely unwritten.
- NVIDIA Efficient AI Lab blog (2026-06-12): R-KV's reported '90% memory savings' were 'measured with pre-allocated contiguous tensors, not in vLLM,' with the developers acknowledging integration is 'a non-trivial challenge.' No general methodology exists for converting reported savings into realized savings under paged attention - the blog fixes it only for its own method.
- llama.cpp perplexity README, stated flatly: 'llama.cpp numbers are not directly comparable to those of other projects because the exact values depend strongly on the implementation details.' There is no accepted cross-stack perplexity normalization, and quantization papers, the HF guide, and llama.cpp use three incompatible conventions (stride 2048 vs 512 vs 512-token chunks scored on the second half).

### Rotation-based quantization for LLMs, with emphasis on the KV-cache branch: QuaRot/SpinQuant-lineage computational-invariance rotations, Hadamard/FWHT transforms, learned and adaptive rotations (SpinQ

**State of the art.** The slice has bifurcated by bit-width, and the frontier has moved decisively AWAY from orthogonality in the last four months.

4-BIT KV: SOLVED AND CLOSED. SAW-INT4 (arXiv:2604.19157, Apr 2026, Tri Dao among authors) is the defining result — token-wise INT4 with BLOCK-DIAGONAL Hadamard rotation is the accuracy/efficiency optimum under real serving constraints (paged layouts, fused attention), recovering nearly all accuracy naive INT4 loses, with a kernel that "introduces zero measurable end-to-end overhead, matching plain INT4 throughput." The defining number: zero overhead, near-full accuracy recovery, deployable. Nothing to contribute here. QuaRot's ladder remains the reference for how KV degrades with bits: at most 0.47 PPL at W4A4KV4 on Llama2-70B; at most 0.21 PPL at 3-bit KV (0.07 on 70B); collapse to 9.23 vs 5.47 baseline at 2-bit on 7B.

2-BIT KV: THE CONTESTED FRONTIER. The number that defines it is RotateKV's (IJCAI 2025) <0.3 PPL degradation on WikiText-2 with LLaMA-2-13B at 2-bit, plus <1.7% on GSM8K, 3.97x peak memory reduction and 2.32x decode speedup. Three 2026 papers now claim to pass it, and all three do so by abandoning data-oblivious rotation: OSCAR (2605.17757, May 2026) with offline attention-aware covariance rotations, ~8x KV reduction and up to 7x throughput on models to 358B; OptR (2608.02691, Aug 2026) with per-head orthogonal corrections learned through the full INT2 attention path plus key centering; and NOVA-KV (2608.04074, Aug 2026), which is the most consequential because it proves the optimal key transform is NOT ORTHOGONAL AT ALL.

THE STRUCTURAL SHIFT. Every 2026 advance in this slice is the same move: replace a storage-space objective with an attention-space objective. OSCAR — Hadamard transforms "smooth the cached tensors, but do not identify which directions are most important for downstream attention." OptR — existing methods use "proxy objectives defined before the complete attention readout" that "do not directly measure the error passed to later layers." NOVA-KV — rotations "equalize variances across entries rather than compacting energy." KVLinC — rotation on keys is worse than no rotation. The RoPE-aligned paper (2608.13365, five days old) closes the loop with a negative result: a provably variance-optimal transform loses on perplexity.

WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE POSITION. The field has independently concluded that the right coordinate is downstream of attention — the same premise as the damage law — but every group

**Collision risk.** Six real collisions, ranked by how much damage each does to the novelty claim. Two of them are three weeks old.

1. AXELRAM (arXiv:2604.02638, 3 Apr 2026, Yasushi Nishida) — SEVERE, AND IT PREDATES THE CAMPAIGN. This paper independently stakes the most distinctive-sounding empirical claim in asset 3. It runs 10 seeds x 3 models on orthogonal-transform KV quantization and reports "sign pattern sensitivity causes catastrophic PPL spikes (Delta > 50) on certain models (Qwen2.5-3B), while others (LLaMA-3.1-8B) are fully stable", explicitly framing this as extending "SpinQuant's observation of rotation variance in weight quantization to the KV cache domain, where the effect is qualitatively more severe", and proposing a per-model remedy. So the tuple {rotation-induced catastrophic KV failure, Qwen2.5 vulnerable, LLaMA immune, model-specific, with a fix} is already on arXiv. What it does NOT have: any noise-response curve, any score-space coordinate, any cross-configuration PREDICTION, 26 models / 13 lineages, held-out bit-widths, or a catastrophe gate. Its stated root cause — layer-wise norm heterogeneity — is different from and shallower than per-head shared key mean, and it never removes the mean or reproduces the failure by injection. ACTION: read it in full before writing a word. The mean-injection experiment either refutes its attribution or subsumes it; either outcome is a strong result, but discovering this paper after submission would be fatal to the framing.

2. OptR (arXiv:2608.02691, 3 Aug 2026) — SEVERE ON THE FIX, SURVIVABLE ON THE DIAGNOSIS. It publishes key centering before rotation with the correct invariance argument: "subtracting the same mu from every key adds a query-dependent constant to all logits and leaves the softmax distribution unchanged." Combined with NSNQuant (NeurIPS 2025), whose "Shift" step is a channel-wise centering placed before the Hadamard transform, mean-removal-before-rotation is now firmly established practice with a year of precedent. Any claim of the form "we discovered that removing the mean fixes it" is dead. What survives is everything upstream: WHY the mean is large, the per-head shared-mean measurement (rho on 16 models), the exact decomposition into k_proj bias at boundary layers plus massive activations in middle layers, the five-generation Qwen diagnosis (immune -> catastrophic at Qwen2 -> immune by Qwen3.5), and on-demand reproduction in an immune model by synthetic injection. Reframe asset 3 as a causal explanation of a known-effective trick, not as the trick.

3. NOVA-KV (arXiv:2608.04074, 4 Aug 2026) — MODERATE, ON THE ATLAS HEADLINE. Its result that "the KLT attains the lowest MSE at every rate, yet ours, with a higher MSE, preserves the top-1 key more often" partially pre-empts "reconstruction metrics do not track real damage." It is not the same statistic — theirs is top-1 key preservation on one comparison, versus Pearson -0.003 between per-vector cosine similarity and end-to-end perplexity across 176 measured cells spanning 26 models — but a reviewer will ask why this is new. The atlas is far stronger evidence and should be presented as such: NOVA-KV shows the decoupling exists in one instance; the atlas measures its magnitude across a population. Cite it as confirming, not competing.

4. HeadQ (arXiv:2605.03562, withdrawn 19 May 2026) — AWKWARD RATHER THAN FATAL, AND CONFIRMED WITHDRAWN. Verified: "Withdrawn by the author because ethical concerns were identified after posting." It publicly disclosed both the score-space coordinate and the score-modulo-constant-shift observation (which is the mean-removal invariance), and claimed "Fisher/score-space error predicts attention KL far better than raw key MSE" across six models. Its numbers are untrustworthy and it cannot be cited as a result, but the abstract page is permanent and discoverable. RECOMMENDED HANDLING: cite it explicitly, note the withdrawal, and state independence. Omitting it costs nothing to find and looks lik

**Open problems (author-stated).**

- SpinQuant (ICLR 2025) leaves the central theoretical question of the slice explicitly open: 'determining if, given an activation distribution with known outlier axes and magnitudes, a closed-form solution for the optimal rotation matrix that evenly distributes magnitude across different axes can be derived.' Two years on, no one has answered it — OSCAR, OptR and HARP all resort to calibration or learning instead.
- SpinQuant's unexplained rotation lottery, still open and now the slice's most cited known-unknown: 'the performance variance is substantial, with the best random rotation matrix outperforming the worst by 13 points', and even structured ones vary — 'random Hadamard rotation matrices exhibit a non-negligible variance in final performance, as large as 6 points.' There is no published method for predicting whether a given rotation is good WITHOUT running end-to-end evaluation. This is a damage-prediction problem stated by the authors, and it is the single best-matched target for the noise-response curve.
- SpinQuant also flags an unexplained activation phenomenon it could not pursue: 'in several activation layers, the first token displays substantial values in multiple channels', suggesting investigating these token-level outliers could 'further enhance quantization accuracy, which could be a potential future research direction.' This is the attention-sink / massive-activation phenomenon that the mean-dominance mechanism decomposes exactly.
- RotateKV (IJCAI 2025) states the hard architectural constraint on adaptive rotation: 'the rotations for each attention head with the same dimension rely on the same Walsh-Hadamard matrix. This constraint limits adaptability to varying channel-wise outlier distributions.' And the reason it cannot simply be fixed: modifying Hadamard matrices 'invalidates the FWHT algorithm, increases computational overhead, and necessitates storing multiple matrices.' Per-head adaptivity versus O(d log d) speed is an unresolved tension.
- RotateKV names the deployment obstacle that blocks every score-space method, including a score-space damage meter: 'efficient attention computation relies on kernels such as FlashAttention, which directly output the attention results without exposing the intermediate attention scores.' Anything requiring attention logits at serving time is currently uncomputable inside a fused kernel. This is a stated, unsolved, high-value engineering problem — and it is squarely addressable on one GPU by a custom kernel or a cheap surrogate computed at prefill.
- RotateKV's only future-work statement is narrow and concedes an unsolved cost: 'Future work will focus on optimize RotateKV's implementation to further reduce the overhead associated with online operation during LLM inference.' Its ablation also exposes an unexplained instability — the 'Reorder + Rotate' combination produces NaN results and 231.43 PPL at 2-bit, a catastrophic failure the authors report but do not diagnose.
- NOVA-KV (Aug 2026) concedes its theory does not cover the regime it targets: 'The high-resolution model might not hold at 2 bits, we use the theory to guide our design rather than as a guarantee.' This is the same structural admission as HIGGS's quadratic model being valid only above ~3 bits — the whole slice's theory is built for a regime above where the interesting damage happens. The low-bit / catastrophic regime remains theoretically unclaimed.
- NOVA-KV explicitly defers the joint problem: 'Joint quantization of keys and values is left for future work.' Every transform-optimality result in this slice treats K and V separately, despite QuaRot showing keys and values have different sensitivities and KVLinC showing they want opposite treatments.
- NOVA-KV reports a decoupling that no one has explained: 'the KLT attains the lowest MSE at every rate, yet ours, with a higher MSE, preserves the top-1 key more often.' They demonstrate MSE does not determine attention fidelity but offer no quantitative model of what does — leaving the predictive question open in exactly the form the damage law answers.
- OptR (Aug 2026) has no limitations or future-work section at all, and its calibration dependence is admitted only in passing: 'Only the rotation parameters are optimized on calibration data. Model weights remain frozen, and the learned rotations are fixed during inference.' Robustness of the learned rotations to distribution shift is untested, and Table 2 shows accuracy still dropping at 64K context even with OptR — long-context degradation is unsolved.
- OSCAR (May 2026) acknowledges its calibration is a dependency rather than a solved detail: all parameters are estimated from 'one offline calibration pass' on a small MMLU-style set, and their own Table 7 probes whether the method is 'sensitive to calibration volume or domain.' A calibration-derived rotation that must be re-derived per model and per domain is a deployment burden nobody in this slice has removed — the data-oblivious property that rotation originally provided has been traded away and not recovered.
- KVLinC (Oct 2025) reports that rotation is counterproductive on keys but does not explain why in mechanistic terms: 'Although Hadamard rotation of keys reduces scaling factor and hence the quantization error with token-wise quantization, it still is much higher than channel-wise quantization of keys', and 'Pre-multiplication mixes tokens prior to quantization, which amplifies quantization noise and injects errors into the attention logits.' The conditions under which rotating keys helps versus harms are undetermined — an open question with a direct, cheap experimental answer.
- The RoPE-aligned paper (Aug 2026) publishes a clean negative result and leaves its cause open: 'The evaluated head-shared pairwise configuration does not improve accuracy in the tested dynamic W4A4KV4 setting. Across four checkpoints, replacing the full-head Hadamard with this configuration increases perplexity at both short and long context lengths.' Their partial account — 'The pairwise transform also has only two-channel mixing support. Along a controlled interpolation from two-channel to full-head mixing, K range, relative quantisation error, and perplexity degradation decrease as support increases' — establishes that mixing support matters but not why variance optimality fails to predict damage.
- QuaRot's stated extensions remain partly unclaimed: 'Opportunities to build on QuaRot include quantizing the residuals and extending the method to mixture-of-experts architectures.' MoE rotation is still thin, and MoE lineages are exactly the kind of architecture a 13-lineage atlas can cover on one GPU where cluster-scale method papers do not bother.
- AXELRAM (Apr 2026) proposes layer-wise norm heterogeneity as the root cause of catastrophic sign-pattern sensitivity but validates on only 3 models x 10 seeds, and its remedy is a 200-candidate search rather than a predictive model. Whether the root cause is norm heterogeneity or something upstream of it is genuinely open — and is directly testable against the mean-dominance hypothesis using the existing rho measurements on 16 models.
- RotorQuant's self-stated limitation, confirmed and worsened by independent replication: 'Block-diagonal rotation (groups of 3) does not decorrelate between groups, potentially limiting MSE on some distributions.' The independent benchmark on Qwen3.6-27B (head_dim 256) found the method inverts entirely — worse PPL AND a larger cache than baseline — concluding 'the approach is not architecture-agnostic.' Cross-head-dimension portability of structured rotations is an open, unstudied failure mode.
- A measurement-validity trap documented in a vLLM integration (larkinwc/vllm-gfx908 PR #70) that no paper in this slice addresses: 'Prompt-logprobs perplexity is structurally blind to KV-cache quantization in this backend: prefill computes attention from freshly-projected K/V; only the decode path reads the quantized cache. A 4-config PPL sweep returned byte-identical results.' Any perplexity-based KV evaluation that measures only prefill is measuring nothing — a silent invalidation risk for wikitext-2 perplexity harnesses generally, and worth auditing in the 176-cell atlas before publication.
