### Token eviction and sparsification for KV-cache compression — i.e. reducing cache cost by *dropping or skipping* tokens/pages rather than by reducing bits per element. Covers (a) the canonical eviction

**State of the art.** There are three separate frontiers, and the honest answer is that the headline numbers do not survive audit.

(1) QUERY-AWARE DECODE SPARSIFICATION — the healthiest frontier. LOCKS (2607.24555, Jul 2026) is the number to beat: FullKV aggregate quality at 100K+ context while attending ~2% of tokens, 2.0x decode latency reduction at 1M tokens, drop-in for unmodified vLLM. It keeps a resident page-local spectral summary at ~10% of cache size, so its true memory story is ~10x, not 50x. Predecessor Quest gave 2.23x self-attention speedup / 7.03x latency.

(2) PREFILL BUDGET ALLOCATION — the most-quoted, least-trustworthy frontier. MixedDimKV (2603.20616) claims LongBench parity at 6.25% cache (16x) and NIAH 100% at 0.26% cache / 50K; LKV (2605.06676) claims near-lossless LongBench at 15% retention; PyramidKV claims full-KV parity at 12%; SnapKV claims 8.2x memory. These are all query-aware numbers.

(3) QUERY-AGNOSTIC REUSABLE CACHE — the deployment-realistic frontier, where numbers collapse to 3-4x. KVzip (2505.23416, NeurIPS'25 Oral) is SOTA: 3-4x reduction (70% eviction), ~2x FlashAttention decode latency, negligible loss to 170K context, on Llama3.1-8B / Qwen2.5-14B / Gemma3-12B.

The defining 2026 result is that (2) is largely illusory. The matched-budget audit (2607.11942, 144,300 paired RULER evaluations) shows that under a query-agnostic protocol only KeyDiff consistently beats a best-of-3 trivial baseline, and SnapKV *loses* to "keep the start and the recent window" by -0.066; per-method degradation is ordered exactly by how visible the query is to each scoring signal. Independently, "Protection Is (Nearly) All You Need" (2605.18053) finds seven policies including H2O, SnapKV, Ada-KV and Quest all collapse to F1<=0.064 without structural protection, and that once 10% of cache is reserved at prompt boundaries the scoring differences become statistically equivalent — recovering 69-90% of ceiling at 13% retention across a ten-model panel. So the defensible state of the art is roughly: ~3-4x query-agnostic with real quality, ~8x with structural protection and modest loss, ~2% of tokens attended at decode if you pay ~10% resident summary, and a hard hallucination cliff near 90% compression (2603.01426).

Note for positioning: nobody in this slice reports wikitext-2 perplexity. Quality is LongBench / RULER / NIAH / IFEval accuracy, and "compression" means retained-cache-budget percentage, not bits per element. A perplexity-denominated damage law is currently

**Collision risk.** Ranked most to least dangerous. Two of these are less than two weeks old.

1. **RippleKV (arXiv:2608.08684, 2026-08-09) — SEVERE, and it is nine days old.** This is the damage-law *mechanism*, published. Verbatim: it 'independently injects norm-adaptive perturbations into each layer's value cache and measures the induced KL divergence at the model output over a small calibration set. Averaging these responses yields a sensitivity profile specific to the model that need not vary monotonically with depth.' Noise injection with no quantizer involved, output-space damage measurement, per-model response profile — the researcher's exact recipe. Surviving differences the paper MUST assert loudly and defend with experiments: (a) RippleKV perturbs VALUES, the researcher perturbs KEYS and reads damage in score space; (b) RippleKV measures KL at a calibration set, the researcher measures end-to-end perplexity across 96 configurations; (c) RippleKV builds a per-LAYER sensitivity ordering, the researcher builds a whole-model curve evaluated at a quantizer's measured noise; (d) decisively, RippleKV ALLOCATES budget, it never PREDICTS the damage of an unseen configuration — no R^2, no held-out bit-widths, no catastrophe gate, no error distribution. If the novelty claim is 'we introduce noise-response calibration for KV cache', that claim is dead. If it is 'we turn noise-response calibration into a validated *predictor* with a quantified miss rate at a safety gate', it survives — but only if stated that way from the abstract onward.

2. **Understanding the Physics of KV Cache Compression (arXiv:2603.01426, 2026-03-02) — SEVERE, multi-asset.** It collides with three assets at once: it frames compression as 'a controlled perturbation of token-level routing'; it finds 'a sharp hallucination safety cliff near 90% compression... indicating a phase transition in semantic reachability' (the catastrophic regime, already claimed for eviction); it reports architecture-specific resilience profiles with LLaMA and Qwen behaving differently (the per-model curve and, uncomfortably, the Qwen-lineage story); and it finds 'moderate compression substantially degrades internal representations while leaving task accuracy largely intact' (cheap proxies decouple from real damage — the atlas's headline). The researcher's cosine-similarity result (Pearson -0.003) is a sharper, quantitative version of a claim that now has a qualitative precedent.

3. **Error Certificates for KV-Cache Eviction (arXiv:2607.21475, 2026-07-23) — SEVERE as a framing threat.** They prove deterministic eviction 'cannot know what it destroyed... no serving-time estimator of that error is consistent', and empirically that 'output log-probability predicts failure better than any cache-side signal'. A reviewer skimming both papers will say the researcher is claiming something proved impossible. It is not the same claim — their impossibility is adversarial and per-request under deterministic *deletion*, while the damage law is average-case, distributional, and about mean-free non-adversarial *perturbation* — but that distinction has to be made explicitly, in the paper, with their result cited by name. Their negative result on cache-side signals is also a live empirical challenge: the researcher should be ready to show score-space noise beats output log-probability as a predictor, or concede the scope.

4. **KVDiagnosis (arXiv:2608.09412, 2026-08-10) — HIGH, eight days old.** Collides squarely with the 176-cell atlas: a 25-method taxonomy, 59,800 compressed runs, per-source FullCache controls so 'no compressor defines another's test set', and ten cheap diagnostics scored against real failures at AUROC 0.684-0.871. That is the same experimental design — many configurations, end-to-end outcome paired with same-forward-pass proxies, proper controls. Mitigations that are real: it is single-model (Qwen3-8B) versus 26 models / 13 lineages, eviction-only versus quantization, and it produces per-row diagn

**Open problems (author-stated).**

- The Sparse Frontier (2504.17768) v1, verbatim: 'Even moderate sparsity levels often result in significant performance degradation on at least one task, highlighting that sparse attention is not a universal solution.' And: 'There is no clear strategy that performs best across tasks and phases, with different units of sparsification or budget adaptivity needed for different scenarios.' The field has no per-task safety criterion — which is precisely the gap a calibrated catastrophe gate fills.
- The Sparse Frontier limitations, verbatim: 'we do not investigate interactions between sparse attention and other model efficiency techniques such as quantisation, weight pruning, or mixture-of-experts sparsity.' The eviction-times-quantization interaction is explicitly unstudied by the field's own largest benchmark. A researcher who owns both a working quantizer and a damage law is unusually well placed to close this.
- The Sparse Frontier future work, verbatim: 'developing reliable sublinear budget allocation mechanisms remains a promising direction' — because they find longer sequences tolerate higher sparsity, so 'fixed-budget methods in production are suboptimal.' Nobody has a principled function mapping context length to safe budget.
- Matched-Budget Audit (2607.11942) limitation #6, verbatim: 'Query-visibility hypothesis remains ordinal evidence; quantitative validation requires continuous sensitivity measures.' This is a direct, dated invitation for exactly the kind of continuous score-space coordinate the researcher already has. Their limitation #5 adds: 'Uniform compression ratios tested; head/layer-adaptive allocation under agnostic protocol unexplored.' Limitation #1: results are at one context length (8192) while 'real deployment occurs at 32k-128k tokens'; #3: 'Only six audited methods from kvpress; KVzip, Compactor, and PyramidKV absent'; #4: H2O's ranking is unresolved because of an attention-backend confound requiring an all-eager re-audit.
- The Pitfalls of KV Cache Compression (ACL 2026), verbatim: 'the true cost of KV cache compression is poorly understood. In fact, the impacts of compression can be very unpredictable. We demonstrate that model performance under compression does not degrade uniformly.' And from the conclusion: 'eviction policies are not able to properly gauge which entries to evict in order to minimize the loss of semantics from the original cache... suggesting new directions for more sophisticated eviction policies that fully unlock the potential of KV cache compression.' They also identify 'a critical range of compression ratios at which models are most vulnerable' — a catastrophic band that nobody has yet turned into a predictor.
- Error Certificates (2607.21475), verbatim: 'Randomization buys attribution, not prediction.' They further report that 'output log-probability predicts failure better than any cache-side signal' — an explicit, measured negative result about cache-side predictors. Prediction of per-request compression failure from cache-side signals is therefore an openly acknowledged unsolved problem, but one they believe may be unsolvable for deterministic eviction.
- Sequential Wyner-Ziv (2605.25085), verbatim: 'Whether recurrent or propagating cache summaries can beat this scaling is left open.' Their converse is proved only within the suffix-only policy class, and their empirical base is four models at 0.5-3B — small enough that generalisation to 7-14B is untested.
- KVDiagnosis (2608.09412): of 12,520 FullCache-correct/compressed-wrong rows, '63.2% have low or partial measured/projected coverage' and only 0.2% combine high coverage with strong likelihood drift, while for 17.0% 'representation fidelity remains unknown'. By the field's own best diagnostic instrument, the large majority of real compression failures currently cannot be explained.
- Physics of KV Compression (2603.01426): moderate compression 'degrades internal representations with little accuracy loss', so internal proxies and task scores decouple in the safe regime; and architectures show 'distinct resilience profiles' (LLaMA early consensus vs Qwen funnel-like late convergence) with no model that predicts which architecture will be fragile before you measure it.
- Practical Online KV Cache Compaction for LLM Agents (2608.00902): for agentic workloads 'immediate compaction often hurts performance, whereas delaying compaction to use the agent's future queries recovers much of the gap' — online compaction under genuinely unknown future queries is unsolved; token eviction preserves most accuracy at 80% KV reduction only when proxy queries are good.
- Expected Attention (2510.00636) names a hard systems constraint the field has not resolved: 'attention scores from future tokens are unavailable during compression, and modern implementations like Flash Attention do not materialize the full attention matrix, making past scores inaccessible.' Any score-space diagnostic intended for serving time inherits this problem.

**Key works.**

- `arXiv:2305.17118` (2023-05-26) **Scissorhands: Exploiting the Persistence of Importance Hypothesis for LLM KV Cache Compression at Test Time** — Introduces the 'persistence of importance' hypothesis — only tokens that were pivotal at one step matter for future steps — and keeps a fixed-budget cache of pivotal tokens. Claims up to 5x KV memory reduction without quality loss, and up to 20x when stacked with 4-bit weight quantization. *Relevance:* The origin of the stack-eviction-with-quantization idea (5x eviction x 4-bit = 20x) that a TurboQuantDC paper would be asked to compare against. Note their 20x is eviction-times-quantization, which is the same multiplicative framing the researcher's 5.54x bit-packing result would be measured against
- `arXiv:2306.14048` (2023-06-24) **H2O: Heavy-Hitter Oracle for Efficient Generative Inference of Large Language Models** — Observes a small set of 'heavy hitter' tokens dominates attention; formulates eviction as a dynamic submodular problem with a theoretical guarantee, retaining a balance of recent and heavy-hitter tokens. Standard operating point in the literature is a ~20% cache budget. *Relevance:* The universal baseline. Also the method the 2026 matched-budget audit had to *withdraw a ranking for* because it requires the eager attention backend — a cautionary tale about backend confounds that directly threatens any cross-method comparison run on one GPU.
- `arXiv:2309.17453` (2023-09-29) **Efficient Streaming Language Models with Attention Sinks (StreamingLLM)** — Shows window attention collapses once text exceeds the cache, and that keeping the KV of a few initial 'attention sink' tokens restores it. Enables stable modeling up to 4M tokens with no fine-tuning. *Relevance:* Two direct ties to the researcher's mechanism asset: attention sinks and massive activations are the same phenomenon family as the per-head shared key mean. Also, StreamingLLM is the 'trivial baseline' that 2026 audits find beats SnapKV under query-agnostic protocols — useful ammunition, and a warni
- `arXiv:2404.14469` (2024-04-22) **SnapKV: LLM Knows What You are Looking for Before Generation** — Uses a 64-token 'observation window' at the end of the prompt to select clustered important KV positions per head. Reports 3.6x generation speed and 8.2x memory efficiency at 16K input, with comparable performance to baseline. *Relevance:* The most widely deployed eviction method and hence the default comparison point — but see collision_risk: two independent 2026 audits show its headline result is an artifact of the query being visible inside its observation window.
- `arXiv:2406.02069` (2024-06-04) **PyramidKV: Dynamic KV Cache Compression based on Pyramidal Information Funneling** — Allocates more cache to lower layers and less to higher ones based on 'pyramidal information funneling'. Matches full-KV performance on LongBench retaining only 12% of the cache; at 0.7% retention beats other methods by up to 20.5 absolute accuracy on TREC; 128 KV entries give Llama-3-70B 100% needl *Relevance:* The layer-adaptive budget idea is the eviction-side twin of the researcher's layer-adaptive bit allocation. Their explicit motivation is massive activations / attention sinks concentrating in higher layers — the same mechanism the researcher decomposed into k_proj bias plus middle-layer massive acti
- `arXiv:2406.10774` (2024-06-16) **Quest: Query-Aware Sparsity for Efficient Long-Context LLM Inference** — Tracks per-page min/max key values, estimates page criticality against the current query, and loads only top-K critical pages. Reports 2.23x self-attention speedup and 7.03x inference-latency reduction with negligible accuracy loss. (ICML 2024.) *Relevance:* Establishes that token criticality is query-dependent — the reason eviction methods that fix a cache before seeing the query behave completely differently from ones that don't. This is the axis on which the whole 2026 audit literature turns.
- `arXiv:2407.11550` (2024-07-16) **Ada-KV: Optimizing KV Cache Eviction by Adaptive Budget Allocation for Efficient LLM Inference** — Derives a theoretical loss upper bound between pre- and post-eviction *attention output*, and uses it to justify the first head-wise adaptive budget allocation. Plug-and-play on top of SnapKV/PyramidKV. Evaluated on 13 RULER and 16 LongBench datasets under both question-aware and question-agnostic p *Relevance:* COLLISION-ADJACENT: Ada-KV already establishes attention-output error (not vector reconstruction error) as the governing quantity for cache compression damage, and turns it into a budget allocator. It is a bound, not a calibrated predictive law, but it pre-dates and partly pre-empts 'the score space
- `arXiv:2505.23416` (2025-05-29) **KVzip: Query-Agnostic KV Cache Compression with Context Reconstruction** — Scores KV pairs by the max attention they receive during a simulated 'repeat the previous context' reconstruction forward pass, then evicts the lowest. 3-4x cache reduction (70% eviction) and ~2x FlashAttention decode latency reduction with negligible loss, contexts to 170K, on Llama3.1-8B / Qwen2.5 *Relevance:* The strongest honest query-agnostic number in the slice (3-4x) — almost exactly the same ratio band the researcher's 5.54x quantizer occupies, so this is the head-to-head a reviewer will demand. Their killer observation: query-aware methods 'suffer performance degradation even at a 90% cache budget 
- `arXiv:2510.00636` (2025-10-01) **Expected Attention: KV Cache Compression by Estimating Attention from Future Queries Distribution** — Computes expected attention scores in closed form from the distributional properties of LLM activations, sidestepping the fact that FlashAttention never materializes the attention matrix. Also releases KVPress, NVIDIA's benchmark library with 20+ KV compression methods and a public leaderboard. *Relevance:* KVPress is the de-facto harness for this slice and the substrate every 2026 audit runs on. If the researcher wants their pre-deployment checker to be adopted, KVPress is the integration target. Also note the practical constraint they name: past attention scores are simply not accessible under FlashA
- `arXiv:2504.17768` (2025-04-24 (v3 revised 2026-06-22)) **The Sparse Frontier: Sparse Attention Trade-offs in Transformer LLMs** — Largest-scale empirical analysis of training-free sparse attention: six methods (Vertical-Slash, FlexPrefill, Block-Sparse, SnapKV, Ada-SnapKV, Quest), multiple model families/sizes, sequences to 128K, sparsity 0.33-0.95, nine tasks. Finds larger sparse models beat smaller dense ones at equal cost;  *Relevance:* The closest thing in this slice to a predictive law, and the paper any 'damage law' submission will be benchmarked against for scope. Its headline negative result — moderate sparsity already breaks at least one task — is the qualitative version of the researcher's catastrophe gate.
- `2026.acl-long.1926 (ACL Anthology; DOI 10.18653/v1/2026.acl-long.1926)` (2026-07 (ACL 2026, pages 41530-41553; Chen, Geh, Grover, Van den Broeck, Israel)) **The Pitfalls of KV Cache Compression** — Evaluates StreamingLLM, SnapKV, TOVA, H2O and K-Norm on Llama3.1-8B and Qwen2.5-14B under multi-instruction prompting with IFEval. Introduces 'degradation curves' per instruction class and 'eviction bias'; shows some instructions are silently and completely dropped; uses system-prompt leakage as a c *Relevance:* Direct precedent for the *shape* of the researcher's contribution: per-configuration degradation curves, non-uniform damage, and a critical/catastrophic band. It is the strongest statement in the literature that aggregate benchmark scores hide the real cost — the same argument the 176-cell atlas mak
- `arXiv:2607.11942` (2026-07-11) **How Query Visibility Changes KV-Cache Compression Rankings: A Matched-Budget Audit** — Matched-budget audit of six kvpress methods (SnapKV, H2O/ObservedAttention, TOVA, ExpectedAttention, AdaKV, KeyDiff) against three trivial baselines (Random, Knorm, StreamingLLM) on three 7-9B models; 144,300 paired evaluations on RULER-8192 and 40,800 on LongBench, ratios r in {0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9}.  *Relevance:* The single most useful paper in this slice for the researcher. It is a solo-scale, matched-budget, many-cell audit that demolishes headline numbers — exactly the genre of the 176-cell atlas — and its stated limitation #6 is an open invitation for the score-space coordinate (see open_problems).

---

### Outlier- and channel-aware KV-cache quantization: the per-channel (Key) vs per-token (Value) axis convention, channel-level outlier isolation and precision boosting, token-level outlier/sink tracing, 

**State of the art.** Two regimes, with different reigning numbers.

3-bit and above (the asker's operating point): KVQuant's <0.1 perplexity degradation on Wikitext-2/C4 at 3-bit (LLaMA/Llama-2/Llama-3/Mistral) remains the reference figure, achieved by per-channel + pre-RoPE key quantization, per-layer sensitivity-weighted non-uniform datatypes, and ~1% per-vector dense-and-sparse outlier isolation. Nobody in this slice has meaningfully improved on that number because attention has moved to 2-bit.

2-bit (where the field actually is, Aug 2026): rotation-based methods have absorbed both the channel and token outlier problems and now define the frontier. OSCAR (Together AI, 2605.17757) sets the deployable bar — 2.28 effective bits, ~8x KV memory reduction, accuracy gap 1.42 points to BF16 on Qwen3-8B and 3.78 on Qwen3-4B, scaling to 358B, with SGLang INT2 kernels and up to ~7x throughput. OptR (2608.02691, three weeks old) is the current accuracy leader by improving OSCAR as a base: Qwen3-8B AIME25 66.00% vs 68.00% BF16 (and 66.67% when applied over QuaRot, up from 17.33%). OScaR (2605.19660) claims near-lossless INT2 with lower complexity via Token Norm Imbalance mitigation, 3.0x decode speedup and 5.3x memory reduction, and defines its Pareto front explicitly against TurboQuant and TurboQuant+.

The axis question is settled as engineering doctrine — per-channel Keys, per-token Values (KIVI) — but is now being routed around rather than answered: rotation-based methods make the axis choice moot by whitening the outlier structure first, and mixed-precision methods (Kitty, MixKVQ, RateQuant) accept the axis and vary bits within it.

Cheap-proxy state of the art: per-channel attention-score MSE under single-channel perturbation (Kitty), gradient-based per-head sensitivity feeding closed-form reverse waterfilling (RateQuant), and Per-Channel Reduction for a three-way safety failure-mode classification (2606.09864). None of these predicts a damage magnitude; all of them rank things.

**Collision risk.** Ranked most to least dangerous. Two assets are in real trouble; two are still defensible.

1. MEAN REMOVAL IS PUBLISHED — THREE TIMES, THE LATEST TWO WEEKS AGO. OptR (arXiv:2608.02691, v1 2026-08-03) reparameterizes keys by subtracting a per-KV-head per-channel calibration mean mu_h before rotation and quantization, with the identical justification the asker uses: 'This key reparameterization is attention-equivalent: subtracting the same mu from every key adds a query-dependent constant to all logits and leaves the softmax distribution unchanged. We apply it only to keys, since shifting values would alter the attention output.' They fuse it into a Triton cache-write kernel by precomputing mu_h R_K offline. Earlier: TaDA (arXiv:2506.04642, 2025-06-05) pairs 'a mean centering to eliminate separate outlier handling' with 'quantization precision that adapts to error sensitivity across layers' — the exact mean-removal + layer-adaptive combination the asker's own memory records as a TurboQuantDC differentiator, published 14 months earlier. And NSNQuant (arXiv:2505.18231, NeurIPS) has channel-wise centering as its explicit middle step between two token-wise normalizations plus Hadamard. VERDICT: mean removal as a mitigation is dead as a novelty claim. What survives is the MECHANISM — none of these three explains why the shared mean exists, decomposes it into k_proj bias at boundary layers plus massive activations in middle layers, traces it across five Qwen generations, or reproduces it on demand by injection. Reframe the asset from 'we remove the mean' to 'we explain and can induce the condition that makes mean removal necessary, and predict which checkpoints need it'.

2. SCORE SPACE AS THE CORRECT COORDINATE IS NO LONGER NOVEL IN THE KV SETTING. Kitty (arXiv:2511.18643, 2025-11-23, Together AI/Microsoft) measures per-channel sensitivity as the MSE between the original and perturbed softmax(QK^T/sqrt(d)) attention score matrix under single-channel INT2 perturbation, reported per query head under GQA. SQuat (arXiv:2503.24358, 2025-03-31, Red Hat) constrains key quantization error to be orthogonal to the query subspace precisely to preserve inner products with future queries. OSCAR (arXiv:2605.17757) picks Q^T Q as its key-side rotation target on the reasoning that 'quantization error in those directions causes larger perturbations to the attention logits'. OptR pushes further downstream to post-W_O output error and explicitly criticises everyone who stops earlier. Even the withdrawn HeadQ (arXiv:2605.03562) claims 'Fisher/score-space error predicts attention KL far better than raw key MSE' across six models. And the popular-press summary at unite.ai already states the folk version — 'measure compression error where the tensor is consumed, not where it is stored' — with numbers (per-channel values: better reconstruction error 3.73 vs 4.57 but 14x worse attention-output error, 49.89 vs 3.55). VERDICT: do NOT claim the score-space coordinate as a contribution. Claim the CALIBRATION: nobody converts a score-space number into a predicted damage magnitude. Every one of these uses score space to RANK (which channel, which rotation, which token) and none of them uses it to ANSWER 'how much worse will this model get'.

3. STRUCTURAL COLLISION WITH THE WHOLE ASSET STACK: Alignment Collapse / PCR (arXiv:2606.09864, v1 2026-06-01, v2 2026-08-08). Eleven models 3.8B-72B, five benchmarks, a training-free per-channel diagnostic from 20 calibration prompts and ~35 GPU-minutes, held-out generalisation to a model from an independent family, an explicit outlier-channel mechanism taxonomy (outlier-crushes-safety / outlier-as-safety / multi-layer dilution), and the headline claim that 'no universal safe bit-width exists, with sharp model-specific phase transitions invisible to standard metrics'. This is the same paper SHAPE as the atlas + damage law + checker, one model-family generation earlier, on a 24GB-feasible budget. Worse, it attacks the damage l

**Open problems (author-stated).**

- OSCAR (2605.17757), Limitations, verbatim: 'Our theoretical analysis explains why the proposed covariance-target rotations are optimal under a frozen-error surrogate with explicit assumptions, but it is not yet a full proof of optimality for the complete autoregressive decoding process. Tightening this gap—for example, by proving when the calibration estimator is unbiased or optimal for the true attention objective—is an important direction for future work.' This is the strongest single opening in the slice: the field's best-resourced 2-bit method admits its calibration-to-true-attention-objective link is unproven, which is exactly the measured relation a validated noise-response curve supplies empirically.
- OSCAR (2605.17757), Limitations, verbatim: 'OSCAR is orthogonal to channel-wise or mixed-precision methods: applying attention-aware/Hadamard rotations inside channel-wise schemes may further improve accuracy, but combining these ideas while preserving an efficient serving layout remains open.' An explicitly stated open composition problem between the rotation family and the channel/mixed-precision family.
- OSCAR (2605.17757), Limitations, verbatim: 'OSCAR uses a fixed bit-width for quantized history tokens and does not study per-token bit allocation or thought-adaptive token precision' and 'we ... do not explore other numerical formats or mixed-format designs, such as NVFP4, different weight precisions, or weight/KV precision combinations, which may create different accuracy–systems trade-offs.' Note this pairs with the asker's held-out result at 5/6/8-bit: bit-widths outside the studied set are an admitted blind spot across the slice.
- OTT / Outlier Tokens Tracing (2505.10938), Limitations, verbatim: 'OTT occasionally still incurs a little loss on specific datasets under 2-bit quantization. This may be related to the difficulty of the datasets and the required generation length. When the generation length is very long, OTT may face an unacceptable risk of loss due to error accumulation.' The authors of an ACL main-conference method cannot say which datasets or which generation lengths are unsafe — a per-model predictive gate with a catastrophe threshold is the literal answer to their stated worry.
- OTT (2505.10938), Limitations, verbatim: 'Due to the presence of the group and nearest tokens, we cannot ensure that all tokens are quantized at every moment. When the sequence length is very short and the batch size is very large, the compression ratio of OTT is reduced. In extreme cases, when the sequence length is shorter than the group size, OTT does not perform any compression.' The gap between nominal and realised compression ratio is an admitted, unquantified problem — and the asker has just been through exactly this (0.17x expansion -> 5.54x real via bit-packing).
- OScaR (2605.19660), Limitations and Future Directions, verbatim: 'our current experiments are primarily conducted on models with LLM backbones ... these models often exhibit architectural characteristics that differ substantially from standard LLM backbones ... We leave a more thorough evaluation across diverse model families to future work.' Also: 'Canalized Rotation requires online computation in the presence of RoPE, which precludes offline fusion of Query and Key Hadamard transforms with weight matrices ... Future work may explore alternative Canalized Rotation that further reduce online overhead.'
- RateQuant (2605.06675), Appendix O, verbatim: 'Our experiments focus on Qwen3 (4B/8B/32B) and Llama3 (3B/8B) model families with WikiText-2 perplexity and standard downstream benchmarks. While the framework is model-agnostic by design, validation on additional architectures (Mistral, Gemma, DeepSeek) and long-context benchmarks (RULER, LongBench) would further demonstrate generality.' Their own listed limitation categories are calibration cost, evaluation scope, the per-head independence assumption, and token-position uniformity — they also write that modelling correlated heads is left to 'future work'. A 26-model / 13-lineage atlas answers the evaluation-scope limitation of the one paper the asker already names as prior art.
- RotateKV (2501.16383), verbatim: 'Future work will focus on optimize RotateKV's implementation to further reduce the overhead associated with online operation during LLM inference.' The rotation family's admitted cost is online transform overhead, not accuracy — which is where a WHT-based cache with real bit-packing can still compete on the systems axis rather than the accuracy axis.
- SQuat (2503.24358), Future work and Limitations, verbatim: 'Investigating whether these latent vectors [MLA compressed latents] can be further quantized, and understanding the impact of quantization errors on model performance, is an interesting avenue for research' and 'Identifying the optimal trade-offs between compression rate and model performance for specific tasks could offer insights into more efficient model deployment strategies.' A direct request for the damage-vs-compression relation the asker has measured — and a flag that MLA/latent-attention architectures are entirely unmapped, which no paper in this slice covers.
- KVQuant (2401.18079), Limitations: the section is entirely about systems (memory allocation for the sparse matrix, prompt-processing vs memory-bandwidth-bound benchmarking, long-context training being orthogonal). The founding per-channel/outlier-isolation paper never states a limitation about knowing in advance whether its configuration is safe on a new model — the gap is a silence, not an admission, which is weaker rhetorically but real.
- Kitty (2511.18643): evaluates only two model families (Qwen3 8B/14B/32B, LLaMA3 8B/70B) on four reasoning benchmarks, and leaves kernel fusion to future work. Its channel-sensitivity metric (single-channel-perturbation attention-score MSE) is presented as an observation with no validation that it predicts end-task damage — the ranking is assumed to transfer, never tested against measured degradation.
- Alignment Collapse / PCR (2606.09864), stated as a finding rather than a limitation: 'no universal safe bit-width exists, with sharp model-specific phase transitions invisible to standard metrics.' The authors assert per-model phase transitions and cheap diagnosis but do not produce a continuous damage predictor; they classify into three mitigation categories. The continuous magnitude question is left open by the paper that most loudly says it matters.
- MixKVQ (2512.19206), stated as motivation: 'current mixed-precision strategies fail to accurately identify components requiring high-precision representation.' A December 2025 paper asserting the field's channel-selection proxies are simply unreliable — corroborating, from inside the slice, the atlas finding that the standard cheap proxies do not track real damage.
- KIVI (2402.02750): states essentially no accuracy limitations, only that fusing the quantization step with preceding operations is left as future work. The axis convention the entire slice inherits was never accompanied by a statement of when it fails — and OTT (2025) and Kitty (2025) both later found that it does, for specific tokens and specific channels respectively. The conditions under which per-channel-K/per-token-V is the wrong choice remain uncharacterised across model lineages.

**Key works.**

- `arXiv:2402.02750 (ICML 2024)` (v1 2024-02-05, v2 2024-07-25) **KIVI: A Tuning-Free Asymmetric 2bit Quantization for KV Cache** — The paper that established the axis convention the whole slice inherits. Empirical study of KV element distributions concludes keys must be quantized per-channel (fixed channels carry very large magnitudes) and values per-token (no consistent outlier pattern). Tuning-free, streaming, with an fp16 re *Relevance:* This is the baseline every reviewer will assume. It is also the quantizer RateQuant, Kitty and PCR all attack, so its 2-bit failure points (PPL 49.3 at 2.5 bits per RateQuant) are the standard example of catastrophic KV damage — exactly the regime the damage law claims to own. Note KIVI states essen
- `arXiv:2401.18079 (NeurIPS 2024)` (v1 2024-01-31, v6 2025-05-28) **KVQuant: Towards 10 Million Context Length LLM Inference with KV Cache Quantization** — Four stacked mechanisms: (i) Per-Channel Key Quantization, (ii) Pre-RoPE Key Quantization, (iii) Non-Uniform KV quantization with per-layer sensitivity-weighted datatypes, (iv) Per-Vector Dense-and-Sparse Quantization isolating ~1% outliers separately for each vector. <0.1 PPL degradation at 3-bit o *Relevance:* The canonical 3-bit reference number the asking researcher's atlas has to beat or contextualise. Its per-layer sensitivity weighting is the closest thing to a damage model in the pre-2025 literature, and it is calibration-fit, not predictive. Its stated Limitations section is purely about systems (m
- `arXiv:2511.18643` (2025-11-23 (Together AI / Microsoft; Xia, Wu, Zhuang, Zhou, Athiwaratkun, Zheng et al.)) **Kitty: Accurate and Efficient 2-bit KV Cache Quantization with Dynamic Channel-wise Precision Boost** — Ranks Key-cache channels by sensitivity and boosts a small fraction (12.5-25%) to 4-bit while the rest stay 2-bit. Crucially, sensitivity is measured IN SCORE SPACE: they quantize a single channel to INT2 with all others unchanged and compute the MSE between the original and perturbed attention scor *Relevance:* THE most direct threat to the 'score space is the correct coordinate' claim inside the KV setting. Published Nov 2025, well before the asker's campaign. Difference that survives: Kitty uses post-softmax attention-probability MSE as a per-channel ranking signal for bit allocation on 2 model families;
- `arXiv:2608.02691` (v1 2026-08-03, v2 2026-08-05 (USC / SNU / Inha)) **Output-Aware Rotation for INT2 KV-Cache Quantization (OptR)** — Argues existing rotation methods 'optimize cache statistics or proxy errors before the complete attention readout' and instead minimises post-W_O attention-output error, decomposed into key- and value-induced terms, learning per-head orthogonal corrections through the full INT2 attention path. ### Scaling laws for quantization and compression — how damage scales with model size (N), bit-width (P), and training data (D); precision-aware scaling laws. Surveyed as a sub-slice of "predicting damage

**State of the art.** There is no single SOTA number, because the slice has two disjoint standards depending on what you are predicting.

FOR PRECISION-AWARE SCALING (predicting loss from N, D, P):
The field standard is Kumar et al. (arXiv:2411.04330, ICLR 2025): a unified L(N,D,P_train,P_post) with delta_PTQ = C_T * e^(-P_post/gamma_post) * (D^gamma_D / N_eff^gamma_N) * prod_x[1-e^(-C_x(P_x-P_post))], fitted on 465 pretraining runs and validated to 1.7B params / 26B tokens. The competing closed form is Ouyang et al. (arXiv:2411.17691, ACL 2025): delta_qLoss = 0.017 * D^0.5251 / (N^0.2261 * P^5.4967), fitted over 1500+ checkpoints. The most general is Panferov et al. (arXiv:2506.01863, NeurIPS 2025): Loss ~ A*(N*rho(R))^-alpha + B*D^-beta + E with a single format-intrinsic capacity rho(R) in (0,1].
Defining characteristics of ALL of these: additive-in-nats damage, weight-centric, coordinates are model/training METADATA (N, D, P) rather than a measurement of the perturbation, fitted from compressed pretraining at sub-2B scale, and no catastrophic regime. None reports an R^2 on end-to-end damage across a x1000 damage range; none is validated on production checkpoints above ~1.7B (Kumar) except by extrapolation.
Their credibility is actively contested: arXiv:2510.06213 (Catalan-Tatjer, Ajroldi, Geiping) argues the shared data-scaling conclusion is 'mostly confounded by the learning rate hyperparameters used in their experiments,' using open trajectories up to 32B / 15T tokens.

FOR KV-CACHE DAMAGE PREDICTION (the cell you actually occupy):
The best published bit-width law is RateQuant (arXiv:2605.06675): D(b) = alpha * beta^-b fitted per quantizer by least squares on ln D vs b, R^2 > 0.99, with beta ranging 3.6 (TurboQuant) to 5.3 (QuaRot). But its D is per-head MSE, its MSE->loss link is an unvalidated second-order Taylor/diagonal-Fisher argument, and it has NO model-size term.
The best published perturbation-theoretic KV result is arXiv:2604.11501: projection damage exceeds quantization damage by 3 * 2^(2b) per direction under the softmax Fisher metric, across five models 124M-14B.
The best published CROSS-MODEL damage-prediction number in the whole survey is arXiv:2608.06564 (2026-08-06): held-out flip rates predicted to median 1.7 points with calibration error 0.004 over 161,744 predictions, across 16 models / 8 families — but only when constants are fitted PER CONDITION; borrowed constants are wrong by 18-33 points at 3 bits. That paper is weight-quantization and decision-mar

**Collision risk.** Be worried. This slice got crowded in the last four months, and three papers landed within two weeks of today.

TIER 1 — MUST READ AND DIFFERENTIATE IN WRITING, OR A REVIEWER WILL DO IT FOR YOU:

1. arXiv:2608.06564, 'Which Decisions Low-Bit Quantization Breaks, and How to Predict Them' (v1: 'Quantization Damage Is Multiplicative, Not Additive'), Wu/Dhiman/Koshiyama, 2026-08-06 — TWELVE DAYS OLD. Independently establishes: damage is MULTIPLICATIVE not additive (statistically defeating additive accounts 378/378); a cross-model study of comparable breadth (16 models / 8 families vs your 26 / 13); held-out prediction with per-condition constants (median 1.7 pts, calibration error 0.004 over 161,744 predictions, no flips used in the fit); and the negative transfer result that BORROWED constants are wrong by 18-33 points. If your paper's abstract says 'damage is multiplicative and the curve must be fitted per model,' this paper already says both. Your surviving deltas: perplexity vs decision margins, KV cache vs weights, MEASURED score-space noise as the independent variable vs bit-width, and the catastrophic regime (they bottom out at margin factor 0.00 at 2 bits; you resolve damage out to x1348). Make the independent-variable difference the headline — 'we predict from a measurement of the perturbation, not from the bit-width' — because that is the one thing they do not do.

2. arXiv:2608.08684, RippleKV, 2026-08-09 — NINE DAYS OLD. Injects norm-adaptive perturbations into the KV cache, measures output KL, builds a per-model sensitivity profile. That is your experimental primitive, published, for KV. It is values-only, KL-based, and used for layer budget allocation rather than damage prediction, so the LAW is still yours — but 'quantizer-free noise injection into the cache to calibrate a model-specific curve' can no longer be claimed as novel methodology. Reframe your novelty as the prediction result (calibrated curve evaluated at a quantizer's measured noise predicts that quantizer's true damage), not the injection.

3. arXiv:2606.09864, Alignment Collapse Under KV Cache Quantization, 2026-06-01. Collides on THREE of your claims simultaneously: (a) 'sharp model-specific phase transitions' in KV quantization damage — the catastrophic regime, already claimed for KV across 11 models 3.8B-72B; (b) a cheap per-model calibrated diagnostic (20 prompts, ~35 GPU-min) that generalizes to a held-out model from an independent family — the same shape as your kvcheck.py + curve; (c) a low-dimensional-subspace mechanism that rhymes with your shared-key-mean collapse. It also carries the sharpest attack on your dependent variable: safety collapses at 1.03x perplexity, so 'perplexity damage' is arguably the wrong thing to predict. Have an answer ready.

4. arXiv:2604.11501, Quantization Dominates Rank Reduction for KV-Cache Compression, Salfati, 2026-04-13. Live (not withdrawn), single-author, 124M-14B, and it formalizes KV damage in SCORE SPACE under the softmax Fisher metric with an explicit bit-width scaling (3 * 2^(2b) per direction). This is the strongest live prior art for 'score space is the correct coordinate for KV damage' — a role the asker currently assigns to HeadQ. Since HeadQ is withdrawn, THIS is the paper that actually threatens that half of the novelty claim. Cite it prominently.

TIER 2 — ERODES SPECIFIC ASSETS:

5. arXiv:2605.06675 RateQuant already publishes a fitted bit-width damage law FOR KV with R^2 > 0.99, and names TurboQuant with beta = 3.6. Your quantizer is already a row in someone's table. It stays in MSE space with an unvalidated Taylor link to loss, and has no model-size term — that is your gap, and it is narrower than it looks.

6. arXiv:2607.28699 WitCert collides with kvcheck.py and with the catastrophe-gate framing: a runtime meter with Lean-4-verified soundness, gating that restores the quality floor, shipped into SGLang with regenerable artifacts. If your checker is positioned as a deployment tool rather than

**Open problems (author-stated).**

- Kumar et al. (2411.04330), Limitations Sec. 6, verbatim: 'the trends we find aim to be suggestive rather than prescriptive, and hope future work can more comprehensively examine these effects at larger model scale.' Also: 'we only consider loss scaling without downstream model evaluations.' Also: 'we use a fixed architecture throughout to examine the effects of precision, parameters, and tokens in a controlled manner.' YOUR OPENING: your atlas is 26 real production checkpoints across 13 architecture lineages up to 14B — precisely the architecture-variation and larger-scale validation they say they cannot do. Their fixed-architecture control is exactly what makes their law blind to your per-head shared-key-mean mechanism, which is an architecture-dependent, generation-dependent property (immune -> catastrophic at Qwen2 -> immune by Qwen3.5).
- Kumar et al. (2411.04330), Appendix D.2, verbatim: 'Making a full scaling law for floating-point quantization is more involved than our integer treatment... We leave a commensurately detailed analysis of mantissa vs exponent... to future work.' Partially answered by 2501.02423 for TRAINING; still unanswered for inference-time KV cache formats (fp8 KV is now the production default in vLLM/SGLang and has no damage law).
- Ouyang et al. (2411.17691), stated limitations: 'The training tokens used in our experiments with Pythia only amount to 300 billion' and 'it is still not extensive enough.' Also that their work covers single-stage pretrained LMs while 'advanced LLMs today often employ multi-stage training strategies including supervised fine-tuning' which 'may cause the behavior... to be significantly different.' They evaluate GPTQ/AWQ/bitsandbytes weight quantization only. YOUR OPENING: your atlas is entirely post-SFT/post-RLHF production checkpoints, which they explicitly flag as an unvalidated regime for their law.
- RateQuant (2605.06675), Sec. 5 Future Work, verbatim: 'The exponential distortion model, while accurate for current quantizers, may require refinement for emerging techniques such as learned vector quantization or sparse-quantized hybrids; characterizing their rate-distortion curves remains an open problem.' Also verbatim: 'Applying the rate-distortion framework to weight quantization and activation compression may reveal similar heterogeneity-driven opportunities.' And: 'extending RateQuant to dynamic allocation during inference could adapt bit-widths based on input characteristics; preliminary experiments suggest that optimal allocations vary by up to 0.5 bits across domains (code vs. natural language).' YOUR OPENING: they admit the model is quantizer-specific and needs refitting per design — a per-MODEL noise-response curve that is quantizer-AGNOSTIC (evaluated at whatever score-space noise the quantizer happens to produce) is the structural fix to their 'distortion model mismatch' failure mode. That is a clean, direct contribution statement.
- RateQuant (2605.06675), admitted breakdown regimes: at <=2 bits recovery drops to ~50% because 'severe quantization noise limits even optimal allocation' and 'the exponential model breaks down when quantization dominates all heads equally'; at >=5 bits the framework is 'degenerate because all heads receive sufficient precision.' YOUR OPENING: both admitted failure regimes are inside your validated range — you have held-out prediction at 5/6/8-bit within x1.5, and you cover damage up to x1348.
- Signal-Noise Perspective (2608.08188), Discussion Sec. 3, verbatim: 'Our analysis focuses on weight-only post-training quantization... The quantitative findings should be interpreted within this scope, rather than directly extrapolated to activation quantization, quantization-aware training, or combined compression.' And: 'the signal-noise framework itself is not tied to a specific quantization method... Future work could extend this framework to other compression paradigms and probe the causal role of source and accumulated errors in downstream degradation.' YOUR OPENING: they hand you the KV/activation extension of their own framework, and your mechanism work (injecting a synthetic shared key component into an immune model to reproduce collapse on demand) is exactly the 'probe the causal role' they call for — you already have the causal verification they say is missing.
- Training-dynamics paper (2510.06213) leaves unresolved the causal mechanism linking loss-landscape geometry to quantization robustness, and generalization beyond dense transformers (Mamba, MoE). It has no formal Future Work section but its whole thesis is that the field lacks a non-confounded predictor of quantization brittleness. That IS the open problem your damage law claims to solve, and it is stated by a well-known group (Geiping) — the single best citation for motivating a measurement-based rather than metadata-based law.
- Wyner-Ziv KV bounds (2605.25085) states verbatim as an explicit open question: 'Whether recurrent or propagating cache summaries can beat this scaling is left open.' Their Theta(eps^(-1/alpha)) result is for suffix-only policies; quantization-based (non-eviction) policies are outside their converse. Relevant if you want an information-theoretic ceiling to place your damage law against.
- WitCert (2607.28699) reports an uncomfortable structural finding you must address: 'aggressive schemes survive on cross-layer error cancellation, not per-step fidelity — in a 28-layer sweep, no single layer's pollution alone loses anything (0/28).' This is an open problem for ANY worst-layer-based coordinate, including yours (worst-layer attention-logit correlation). Either your worst-layer statistic is a proxy for an accumulation that is genuinely distributed, or you have a real disagreement with a Lean-verified analysis. Resolving it is a contribution in itself.
- Alignment Collapse (2606.09864) implicitly opens the biggest problem for your framing: it demonstrates safety alignment collapsing at 1.03x perplexity, i.e. perplexity damage near zero while a downstream capability is destroyed. Nobody has a law predicting NON-perplexity damage from perturbation. Your damage law's dependent variable is perplexity; extending your curve to a second, capability-space damage axis is both the obvious next contribution and the obvious reviewer objection.
- arXiv:2608.06564 states its own central limitation as a finding: borrowed constants are wrong by 18-33 points at 3 bits, so the relation must be calibrated per condition. Nobody has explained WHY the constants differ across models, or predicted them from measurable checkpoint properties. Your mechanism asset (per-head shared key mean / rho mean-dominance measured on 16 models) is a candidate answer to exactly that question — predicting a model's curve parameters from a cheap structural measurement, rather than fitting them, would be a genuinely new result in this slice.

### Calibration-free and normalization-based KV-cache quantization — specifically the "who centers/shifts keys before quantizing, and what do they claim for it" question. The slice splits cleanly into fou

**State of the art.** Two frontiers, and you should quote both.

CALIBRATION-FREE (your slice proper). KVarN (arXiv:2606.03458, Huawei, June 2026) is the current SOTA and the number that defines it is 2-bit generative-benchmark accuracy at ~2.3 bits/element with a shipping vLLM backend: Qwen3-4B AIME24 60.0 vs KIVI 55.5; Phi-4-14B MATH500 84.8 vs KIVI 74.4 and HumanEval 88.2 vs 74.6, at 0.18% quantization overhead. Its mechanism is Hadamard + Sinkhorn-style dual-axis variance normalization with NO centering. NSNQuant (NeurIPS 2025) remains the reference point for calibration-free VECTOR quantization and for the centering question specifically: LLaMA2-7B WikiText-2 PPL 5.285 at 2.23 average bits, and — the single most useful number in this whole survey — 5.842 with the channel-wise centering step removed. That 0.557 PPL is what the field currently believes key centering is worth in a calibration-free quantizer.

CALIBRATED (where centering has moved in the last three weeks). NOVA-KV (arXiv:2608.04074, 4 Aug 2026) and OptR (arXiv:2608.02691, 3 Aug 2026) both center keys, both invoke softmax offset-invariance, and both define the frontier at ~2.3 BPE on Qwen3/Llama-3.1/GPT-OSS: NOVA-KV hits 75.4% RULER NIAH at 128K on Qwen3-8B (BF16 83.4, OSCAR 25.3) and 54.0% on GPT-OSS-20B where OSCAR scores 0.0. OptR's isolated key-reparameterization ablation is +2.67 points on AIME25 (Qwen3-4B-Thinking: 63.33 -> 66.00). AATC (arXiv:2608.14191, 14 Aug 2026) sets the theory bar with a proof that white-noise quantization distortion decomposes additively into attention-visible key and value terms, reaching ~5.8x near-lossless.

For the metric question, the defining published position is OSCAR's: logit distortion is tr((K-K_hat) Q^T Q (K-K_hat)^T), 'controlled by the query covariance Q^T Q rather than by K^T K alone' — i.e. score space, argued from theory and validated as a four-stage error chain, but never turned into a per-model predictive law.

**Collision risk.** Be blunt with yourself: this slice has gotten crowded in the last 90 days, and two papers from the first week of August 2026 hit you hard. Ranked by severity.

SEVERE — KEY CENTERING IS FULLY OCCUPIED, INCLUDING THE INVARIANCE ARGUMENT.
1. SageAttention (arXiv:2410.02367, ICLR 2025) published gamma(K) = K - mean(K) with the exact identity sigma(q(K-mean(K))^T) = sigma(qK^T - q.mean(K)) = sigma(qK^T), an overhead figure ('<0.2%'), and a large recovery number (FID 221.18 -> 166.52), in October 2024. Any sentence of the form 'we observe that removing the shared key mean is free because softmax is shift-invariant' is dead on arrival.
2. NSNQuant (arXiv:2505.18231, NeurIPS 2025) is calibration-free KV quantization whose middle step IS per-channel key centering, with a clean ablation (2.23-bit LLaMA2-7B WikiText-2: 5.285 with, 5.842 without). Your mean-removal component is prior art in exactly your setting.
3. OptR (arXiv:2608.02691, 3 Aug 2026) publishes per-(layer,head,channel) key centering, calls it 'attention-equivalent', proves nothing changes in softmax, and gives a standalone ablation on Qwen3 — the lineage you study. Fifteen days old.
4. TaDA (arXiv:2506.04642, ACL 2025 Industry) already pairs mean-centering with layer-adaptive precision — the exact combination your project notes describe as the TurboQuantDC differentiator.
Verdict: mean-removal is a re-implementation, not a contribution. Do not lead with it. The narrow strip that survives: NSNQuant and TaDA both STORE and restore the offset (paying bits for it) rather than exploiting the invariance to drop it; OptR and NOVA-KV exploit the invariance but need calibration. Calibration-free + drop-the-mean-entirely + folded into W_Q/W_K at prefill (the InnerQ trick, arXiv:2602.23200) is a genuinely vacant cell, but it is an engineering cell, not a paper.

SEVERE — SCORE SPACE AS THE CORRECT COORDINATE IS NO LONGER YOURS ALONE.
5. NOVA-KV (arXiv:2608.04074, 4 Aug 2026) makes attention-product error the objective, derives closed-form transforms from it, AND shows the criterion preserves top-1 attention agreement better than MSE. Fourteen days old.
6. OSCAR (arXiv:2605.17757) states the logit-distortion identity tr((K-K_hat) Q^T Q (K-K_hat)^T) and validates a full chain from cache MSE to attention-score KL to hidden-state MSE, arguing reconstruction error is the wrong metric.
7. HeadQ (arXiv:2605.03562, withdrawn) claimed BOTH score space and shift-invariance in one clause — 'the visible object is score error modulo constant shifts' — and claimed that score-space error 'predicts attention KL far better than raw key MSE' across six models. Withdrawn for authorial ethical reasons, not for being wrong; a reviewer who knows it will remember.
Verdict: 'score space is the right coordinate' is now a defended position with multiple 2026 papers. What is still unoccupied is score space used PREDICTIVELY on end-task damage: NOVA-KV explicitly declines the guarantee, OSCAR stops at illustration, HeadQ stops at attention KL. Your chain must be sold as noise -> score-space correlation -> PERPLEXITY MULTIPLIER, and the perplexity leg is the only leg that is yours.

8. AATC (arXiv:2608.14191, 14 Aug 2026 — four days old) proves that under a WHITE-NOISE quantization model the expected attention-aware distortion decomposes additively across keys, values, tokens and channels. That is the analytic version of your empirical noise-injection chain. It stops at reverse-water-filling bit allocation and never calibrates per model, never predicts perplexity, and — decisively for you — a small-perturbation white-noise model cannot describe a x1348 blow-up. Cite it and say so explicitly.
9. Alignment Collapse (arXiv:2606.09864, v2 8 Aug 2026) is your paper's silhouette on a different axis: 11 models, 'no universal safe bit-width exists, with sharp model-specific phase transitions invisible to standard metrics', named mechanistic failure modes,

- NSNQuant (arXiv:2505.18231), Appendix I.1 — the NSN standardization provably fails in early layers: 'This is due to the presence of outlier channels with huge variances in the first layer, which enlarges the bound Gamma. Despite this limitation, Figure 5 demonstrates that the quantization error remains low in these layers as well.' And explicitly: 'We leave it to future work to explicitly model and account for these exceptions.' Your layer-adaptive work plus per-layer noise curves answer exactly this — the layers where a distributional assumption breaks are the layers a measured curve handles without assumption.
- NSNQuant — the paper 'uses the same precision for all tokens, but future work could incorporate token importance as in hybrid approaches.' Combined with the previous item, NSNQuant openly lacks any allocation principle across layers or tokens. It is the highest-profile calibration-free centering method and it has no answer to 'how many bits where.'
- NSNQuant — model coverage is LLaMA2-7B/13B, LLaMA3-8B, LLaMA3.1-8B and Mistral-7B-v0.3 only. No Qwen, no analysis of GQA versus MHA, no attention-sink discussion, and no stated explanation for why per-channel key means are large in the first place. A centering method that never examines the origin of the thing it removes is an open door.
- TaDA (arXiv:2506.04642), Limitations verbatim — 'Our approach relies on using search to find the right quantization precision per layer to achieve appropriate compression. However for each task, we currently make use of a sub-sampled training set that belongs to the same domain but does not contain the same data samples as in the evaluation benchmarks. Such task dependent customization adds some practical challenges for general and scalable deployment. A data-agnostic search or a universal golden dataset for the search would be an interesting solution to this problem but that is left for future exploration.' This is the single most direct invitation in the slice: a per-model damage law calibrated with quantizer-free noise IS the data-agnostic replacement for their per-task random search.
- KVarN (arXiv:2606.03458), Limitations verbatim — 'Some novel LLM architectures do not require a KV-Cache (e.g. state-space models SSMs). Our method is not suitable for such architectures. Some recent models use train-time compression for the KV-Cache in the form of multi-head latent attention (MLA). As in other KV-Cache compression methods, it is unclear how such attention mechanisms affect quantization quality. End-to-end evaluation on a publicly available serving framework is hindered by the fact that currently such frameworks do not support 2-bit KV-Caches.' Note the middle clause: MLA behaviour under quantization is admitted to be unknown by the current SOTA holder, and your 13-lineage atlas is the instrument for settling it.
- NOVA-KV (arXiv:2608.04074) — no formal limitations section, but concedes 'The high-resolution model might not hold at 2 bits', 'we use the theory to guide our design rather than as a guarantee', and 'Joint quantization of keys and values is left for future work.' Appendix J.2 documents baseline orthogonal methods (OSCAR, QuaRot) failing outright on GPT-OSS-20B under hybrid attention, so architectural sensitivity is openly unsolved. - FibQuant (arXiv:2605.11478), Limitations verbatim — 'The theory identifies the canonical source and the high-rate vector gain, but finite-rate Lloyd-Max optimality for every practical (d,k,N) is not characterized in closed form. Larger models, longer contexts, bootstrap confidence intervals over rotations and prompts, and fused-kernel implementations are natural next steps.' Their strongest empirical claim against TurboQuant rests on a 1.1B model.
- TurboAngle (arXiv:2603.27467), Limitations verbatim — 'We evaluate perplexity on WikiText-2 only; downstream task accuracy and long-context benchmarks (e.g., LongBench) remain untested. Runtime overhead of the FWHT encode/decode path has not been measured under realistic batch and sequence sizes. The uniformity argument is asymptotic in d; finite-dimension errors may affect models with very small head dimensions (d<32).' Also concedes its per-layer early-boost 'requires three to five evaluation runs per model' — brute-force sensitivity search that a predictive law replaces.
- InnerQ (arXiv:2602.23200) — 'Future work will systematically examine how our quantization scheme interacts with alternative KV cache eviction strategies', plus a request for 'adaptive and low-overhead policies for selecting tokens retained in high-precision windows', and an admitted 0.5 bits/element wasted on zero-point overhead despite 99% mask sparsity. Note also it provides NO standalone ablation for its per-channel key normalization — the component nearest to centering is unmeasured even by its own authors.
- VecInfer (arXiv:2510.06175) — 'First, while combining vector quantization with sparse attention patterns for mixed-precision KV cache compression is a promising approach, the trade-offs between accuracy and efficiency remain to be thoroughly explored. - HQMQ (arXiv:2605.27646), Limitations verbatim — '(i) HQMQ does not compete with CommVQ in the sub-3-bit / 1-bit territory: radius quantization breaks below 2 bits and free-magnitude variants fail empirically. (iv) Long-context retrieval is limited to needle-in-a-haystack at 4k/8k with n<=25... (v) We did not evaluate HQMQ composed with KV-eviction methods.' Future work asks for 'attention-aware bit allocation across heads and layers' — again, an allocation problem with no allocation principle. - KVSink (arXiv:2508.04257) — no formal limitations section, but states: 'A notable observation is the occurrence of unusually large norms in the Queries and Values of sink tokens in the final layer, a phenomenon that has not been fully explored in previous research. - OptR (arXiv:2608.02691) — contains NO dedicated Limitations or Future Work section at all, and cites no prior work for key centering despite SageAttention having published the identical identity in 2024. - OSCAR (arXiv:2605.17757) — no standalone limitations section; the acknowledged weaknesses are that calibration is dataset-specific and that it degrades badly at 128k on smaller models (Qwen3-4B RULER 39.5% versus 81% BF16). The stated design philosophy — that attention-consumption statistics rather than reconstruction error control accuracy — is asserted and illustrated by a four-stage error chain, but never turned into a quantitative predictor.
- Quantized Keys Steal Attention (arXiv:2605.26266, Tuncer/Becker/Pfeil, 2026-05-25) — assumes a 'zero-mean, approximately uniform quantization-noise model', works best 'when cached attention is spread over enough tokens', notes single-token decoding gives 'less headroom for the correction', and leaves floating-point formats (FP, MXFP, NVFP) as 'an open direction'. Their bias term b_i = log E[e^{delta_i}] ~= sigma_i^2/2, i.e. half the SCORE-SPACE noise variance, is the closest published quantity to your score-space noise coordinate — and it is derived, not measured, and primarily for video diffusion.

### llama.cpp / ggml KV-cache quantization: the `-ctk`/`-ctv` (and `-ctkd`/`-ctvd` draft) flags, which ggml types are legal for the cache, what backend constraints gate them, which model families are know

**State of the art.** There is no algorithmic state of the art in llama.cpp — the shipped quantizers are the same legacy 2023-era block formats used for weights (q8_0 = 32-element blocks, 8-bit + fp16 scale; q4_0 = 32-element blocks, 4-bit + fp16 scale), applied unchanged to cache rows. The only algorithmic advance in two years is PR #21038 (merged 2026-04-01): a free, deterministic Hadamard rotation of Q/K/V, auto-enabled whenever the cache is quantized and head_dim % 64 == 0. Everything else that was proposed — TurboQuant, PolarQuant, E8 lattice, TQ3_0 — was rejected.

The defining numbers, in the ecosystem's own units:
- DEFAULT AND SAFE: q8_0/q8_0. On Qwen2.5-7B, mean KLD 0.001782, PPL ratio 1.0042, same-top-p 98.0% (discussion #23470). Johannes Gaessler's 2024 verdict still stands: "no significant quality loss from using q8_0 instead of FP16."
- BEST QUALITY PER BYTE: q8_0-K + q4_0-V (6.5 BPV), KLD 0.004766, 96.7% top-p — but unreachable on a stock CUDA build, which only compiles symmetric K/V pairs, and forbidden outright on MLA/DeepSeek architectures. ik_llama.cpp's variant is q8_0-K + iq4_nl-V at the same 6.5 BPV.
- THE CLIFF: q4_0/q4_0 (4.5 BPV). This is where the ecosystem has no coherent story. Llama-2-7B: PPL ratio ~1.03 (PR #7412). Gemma3-4B: 7.79 vs 7.69, ~1.01 (PR #21038). Qwen3.5-4B: 8.3448 vs 8.3266, ~1.002. Qwen3.8-27B: production-viable at 256K on 24 GB (discussion #27290). But Qwen3-0.6B: 46.25 vs 13.67, x3.4 AFTER rotation (x4.5 before). And Qwen2.5-7B: PPL 1589.9 vs 7.96, x199.7, measured with rotation active. Two orders of magnitude of spread across models at the same flag, with no published explanation and no way for a user to know in advance which side of it their model is on.

Best available prediction method today: none. The state of the art in "will -ctk q4_0 hurt my model" is to run `llama-perplexity --kl-divergence-base X.dat --kl-divergence -ctk ... -ctv ...` once per configuration per model — an hour-scale full end-to-end evaluation with no transfer between configurations, no transfer between models, and no early-warning signal. The documentation offers zero guidance.

**Collision risk.** Nothing in this slice does what your damage law does. But three items dent specific novelty claims, and one is serious.

SERIOUS — the rotation is upstream and free (PR #21038, merged 2026-04-01, by the project lead). Mainline llama.cpp has applied an orthonormal Hadamard rotation to Q/K/V before cache quantization for four and a half months. Consequences for you: (a) any framing of your production cache as novel because it rotates before quantizing is finished in the systems context — a reviewer will say ggerganov shipped that in one weekend, backend-agnostic, at "almost 0 cost", explicitly to pre-empt TurboQuant PRs; (b) his PR body contains a 4-model x 5-bit-width before/after PPL table that is a miniature of your atlas and already exhibits your family pattern (Qwen3-0.6B catastrophic, Qwen3.5-4B immune, Gemma3 benign). Your compression ratio and your Lloyd-Max/residual-sign stages are untouched by this, and so is the entire prediction contribution. But cite #21038 explicitly and position against it, do not let a reviewer find it first.

REAL — the K-side catastrophe is publicly measured (discussion #23470, 2026-05-21). Qwen2.5-7B, q4_0-K: PPL 1589.9 vs 7.96, mean KLD 5.509, same-top-p 11.6%, with the crisp attribution "butchered; is it all in the K?" and "V at 4-bit cost nearly nothing". That is your headline phenomenon — a x200 catastrophe, entirely K-side, on a Qwen2 lineage model — in public, dated, and rigorous. It is one model with no mechanism, no coordinate, no law and no gate, so it does not touch your contribution; it is a data point your law explains. Treat it as a validation exhibit, not a threat, but do not claim first observation of the phenomenon.

REAL — the family-tolerance narrative is staked (issue #21385, 2026-04-03). SCJedi publicly claims q4_0 KV is "completely lossless on hybrid models (Qwen3.5)" at BLEU 1.000 and attributes tolerance to architecture. That collides with the narrative shape of your mechanism section, though not its content: his causal story is "linear/sliding attention layers act as error correction", yours is a shared per-head key mean. These are distinguishable and yours is falsifiable against his — inject a synthetic shared component into a hybrid model and see whether the linear layers save it. Doing that experiment converts the collision into a differentiator. Also flag issue #24139 / KVarN (arXiv:2606.03458): "quantization errors ... driven primarily by incorrect token scales" is the nearest published mechanism claim, per-token magnitude rather than per-head mean, and reviewers will ask you to distinguish them.

MINOR — PR #25352 (E8 lattice, closed) explicitly assumes "per-head variance homogeneity that holds after Hadamard rotation". That is an assumption your mechanism contradicts rather than a competing result, but it means a third party has already reasoned publicly about post-rotation per-head statistics.

CLEAR — I searched this slice specifically for each of your four assets and found nothing. No noise-injection calibration of any kind (no quantizer-free perturbation study, no noise-response curve, no transfer of a curve across bit-widths). No score-space / attention-logit-correlation coordinate — the only proxies anyone uses are PPL, KL divergence, same-top-p, BLEU and draft acceptance. No cross-model atlas: the largest published sweep is ggerganov's 4 models, and the largest per-model sweep is Gaessler's 45 rows on ONE model. No damage prediction of any sort, and no catastrophe gate. No pre-deployment checker — nothing in llama.cpp or ik_llama.cpp resembling kvcheck.py; the closest is PR #21586, which merely checks that flash attention is enabled. No published rho / mean-dominance measurement.

STRATEGIC RISK, not a citation collision: the upstream door for a new cache type is shut. Verified — every KV-quantization type PR since March 2026 was closed unmerged (#21131, #21241, #21307, #21789, #21818, #22441, #23617, #24049, #24050, #25352), several auto-flagged "fully or

- ggerganov, PR #21038 (the merged rotation), verbatim: "I don't know what is the impact of the remaining techniques explained in TurboQuant (PolarQuant, QJL, etc.). They could be important and can potentially improve further on top of this." The project lead is on record that the second stage of exactly your algorithm is unevaluated in llama.cpp.
- ggerganov, same PR: "Only based on the PPL data below, I think this should never be worse that before, though it needs a bit more evaluation." A merged, always-on-by-default transform whose author states it is under-evaluated — and whose own table shows it leaves Qwen3-0.6B q4_0 at x3.4 damage.

Unexplained and unfixed.

- Issue #26989 (OPEN, 2026-08-13) rests on an entirely untested premise: "integer quantization often struggles to preserve fine-grained attention weights in long-context reasoning and code generation tasks. FP4 formats offer a significantly better dynamic range." No one has measured INT4 vs FP4 for KV in this ecosystem.
- Discussion #27290 (2026-08-18), on drafter/target quantization alignment: "Is drafter-target quantization alignment something anyone else has measured? I did not find prior numbers." A published, unanswered request for prediction on a 24 GB GPU.
- Documentation: verified on master today, the complete quality guidance for -ctk/-ctv across README.md, docs/build.md and tools/server/README.md is the list of legal type names. Issue #10373 asked for this in 2024 and was closed by printing the list. Nothing warns that quantized V silently forces flash attention, or that a Hadamard rotation is silently switched on and can send an architecture off its intended attention path (issues #25382, #25725).
